# Lean formalization findings

This is the durable findings ledger for `a12-kernel-lean`, adapted from a12-dmkits' separation between kernel truth, implementation treatment, and live gaps. It records settled conclusions about how this project should formalize, prove, and empirically anchor the project-owned semantics. Language-neutral behavior belongs in [`../spec/`](../spec/); this ledger owns its Lean consequence and does not become a second semantic specification or backlog.

Each finding has a stable `LF<n>` identifier, date, semantic section, evidence basis, Lean treatment, and explicit limit. Admit a finding only when a durable non-obvious mechanism, representation choice, semantic surprise, or rejected stronger account will affect later work. Routine capsule closure, gate results, coverage status, and next steps belong in [`IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md`](IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md), [`PLAN.md`](PLAN.md), or Git history. Findings are not deleted or renumbered; later corrections amend the existing record visibly.

## Query contract

Do not cold-read this ledger. Search the stable `LF<n>` heading when another owner cites it, search `Sections:` for a canonical `§n`, or search the mechanism name and then read only the complete matching finding. Useful forms are `rg -n '^## LF|Sections:.*§5' docs/LEAN-FINDINGS.md` and `rg -n -i 'fillability|reopened-star|processing context' docs/LEAN-FINDINGS.md`. The metadata line is the index; a second topic table would duplicate it and drift as findings are amended.

## LF1 — differential evidence and proofs answer different questions

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: cross-cutting. Basis: project decision; Cedar-style executable-spec/production-engine separation; the local a12-dmkits (`a12-rulekit/`) corpus and differential architecture.

Kernel differential testing is the empirical backbone. Each executable Lean capsule must ultimately receive applicable retained portable observations before it is described as kernel-correspondence complete, but related clauses may be calibrated together and an internal capsule may land honestly as `external evidence pending`. Proofs establish universal consequences of the chosen Lean definitions; they do not establish that the external kernel implements those definitions. Fuzzing broadens the empirical search but proves neither internal laws nor universal correspondence.

The evidence topology has four distinct roles: the real kernel is behavioral authority; current a12-dmkits corpus and differential facilities provide focused kernel-facing observations when their existing shape fits, while a missing observation requires a concrete purpose-specific handback rather than an assumed generic exporter; compact observations remain checked out for ordinary Lean replay while historical raw audit units stay recoverable at pinned Git revisions with project-local archive records; the a12-dmkits interpreter is a clean-room peer for triangulation and divergence discovery, never the oracle. Contrary kernel evidence overrides agreement between Lean and the interpreter.

Lean treatment: every live fragment in [`IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md`](IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md) separates internal assurance from external adequacy. A capsule without retained kernel observations is marked `external evidence pending`, even when it builds, executes, and has trusted proofs.

Limit: finite retained observations cannot prove universal kernel equivalence. A wrong Lean primitive can support flawless theorems, and a finite differential suite can miss a divergence; confidence comes from keeping both forms of evidence connected without collapsing them.

## LF2 — checked elaboration must own model and runtime-policy coherence

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: §2, §10. Basis: [`../spec/08-paths-and-references.md`](../spec/08-paths-and-references.md), read-only path differentials, Lean/Lean4Lean parser–elaborator separation, and `do` Unchained's explicit surface-to-core transformation pattern.

The flat evaluator originally accepted resolved IDs, typed field metadata, and already checked cells independently. That was a real semantic admission: a caller could pair one identifier with inconsistent kind/scale metadata or check its raw cell under a different policy.

Lean treatment: [`A12Kernel/Elaboration/Flat.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/Flat.lean) introduces a parser-independent surface subset and an expanded `FlatModel`. Resolution validates the model first, never uses first-match ambiguity, rejects repeatable scope, and lowers only legal scalar conditions into `CheckedFlatCondition`. The wrapper carries both model-validity and core-field well-formedness proofs. `FlatModel.checkContext` applies the same unique declaration policy to raw cells; unresolved or ambiguous IDs fail closed as malformed.

[`A12Kernel/Proofs/Elaboration.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/Elaboration.lean) proves that an admitted field has one matching non-repeatable declaration, that raw-to-checked context construction uses exactly its `formalCheck` policy, and that lookup failure observes as validation `unknown .malformed`. This closes policy coherence for the checked surface route. The low-level core evaluator remains intentionally usable with an arbitrary `FlatContext` for isolated semantic laws, so the stronger claim does not apply to arbitrary direct calls.

Supported flat path treatment, corrected by LF6 and amended on 2026-07-23: structured absolute paths, parent-relative paths with an optional explicit turning-point label, and bare lookup in the kernel order declaring group → flag-gated model-wide unique name. Parent/ancestor lookup requires explicit `..`; it is not a hidden bare-name tier. The retained turning-point label checks only the group reached by the parent count and cannot change that count or search another ancestor. The same guard is reused by group-valued and later-star paths. A quote-aware structured adapter now retains authored single-quote provenance for every already-tokenized name position, requires it only for exact membership in an injected selected-language terminal profile, and erases accepted quotes before the unchanged resolver. Canonical reification quotes exactly those terminals and is a proved left inverse; unnecessary quotes remain legal. A later narrow amendment reuses the declaration resolver and parent walker for one group-qualified direct-child star and correlated `Having`: [`A12Kernel/Elaboration/Correlation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/Correlation.lean) requires an explicitly declared repeatable group path, exact singleton scope, Number kind, supported comparison, both origins, and operator-specific scale legality before producing `CheckedSingleCorrelatedRule`. This is one closed lowering route, not general star or `$` syntax.

External limit, amended on 2026-07-23: [`ShortNameRefDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/ShortNameRefDiffTest.kt) and [`NamedAncestorPathDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/NamedAncestorPathDiffTest.kt) establish broad kernel-backed provenance. The separating flat bundle covers explicit parent match/mismatch, absolute lookup, declaring-group precedence, unique model-wide fallback, duplicate rejection, and flag-disabled rejection. Structured named turning points retain the authored label and lock matching transparency versus mismatching rejection across flat, group, and later-star routes. Kernel lexer/formatter source plus maintained a12-dmkits `PathPolicyTest`, `PathLawsTest`, and `QuotedPathSegmentParseTest` establish the quote decision; Lean locks keyword fields/groups, exact case, unnecessary quotes, quote erasure, and resolver delegation. The correlation-elaboration bundle adds one exact star path and three static rejection classes plus an accepted mixed-scale ordering control. LF6 records the bare-name correction. Concrete bilingual tokenization, lexical name admission, dot-component rendering, full condition roundtrip, `RuleGroup`, semantic indices, wider nested or multiple-star consumers, and general repeatable paths remain outside the admitted fragment rather than untested claims within it.

## LF3 — `$` correlation requires two explicit environments

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: §9, §10, §12. Basis: [`../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md`](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md), [`../spec/01-data-model.md`](../spec/01-data-model.md), read-only kernel codegen/runtime source, a12-dmkits findings and interpreter treatment from the `a12-rulekit/` checkout, and retained kernel differentials.

The semantic mechanism is an explicit two-environment filter. A rule begins with a complete captured repetition environment `outerEnv`. A starred operand enumerates candidate rows and constructs an `innerEnv` for each candidate. Inside `Having`, ordinary references resolve against `innerEnv`, while `$`-marked references resolve against `outerEnv`; the reference's resolved repeatable level then selects its own coordinate. `CurrentRepetition(G)` and `CurrentRepetition($G)` therefore mean “read level `G` from the candidate or captured environment,” not “read one universal inner or outer row.” `$` is correlation, not positional selection.

The kernel source exposes the same mechanism at its data-flow boundary. [`IterationState.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/IterationState.java) carries the complete repetition-index vector plus the captured outer state, [`EbenenIterator.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/EbenenIterator.java) pairs that capture with each candidate while evaluating the filter, and [`RtInternalIdentifier.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/RtInternalIdentifier.java) first chooses captured or candidate state from the outer marker and then resolves every named path level from that state. [`RuntimeController.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) resolves an empty Number to zero with fillability metadata and obtains `CurrentRepetition` from the resolved identifier's last repetition index; the document API states that repetition indices are 1-based in [`PathPart.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-document-v2/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/document/apiV2/PathPart.java). A formally invalid field is not check-relevant, and [`BedingungsOperatorHelper.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/BedingungsOperatorHelper.java) maps a comparison reading such an operand to the kernel's collapsed false-or-unknown result. [`CodeGenFilterCondition.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/conditionsDir/CodeGenFilterCondition.st), [`CodeGenCondition.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/conditions/CodeGenCondition.java), and [`ConditionSuffix.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/conditionsDir/ConditionSuffix.st) show that a generated `Having` predicate keeps only a known true result.

Star binding is inseparable from this mechanism. Bindings are retained only above the first star; a same-group star reopens that group and spans all its instantiated rows rather than silently collapsing to the current row; a multi-star path binds at the first star. The initial Lean reference meaning must be a straightforward candidate-row filter, not the interpreter's equality-bucket/hash-join optimization.

The most important edge cases are already exposed by the read-only evidence estate:

- Self-match is real. `[inner] == [$outer]` includes the outer row unless the authored filter explicitly adds `CurrentRepetition(G) != CurrentRepetition($G)`; the evaluator must not infer self-exclusion. See [`CorrelationLawsTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/CorrelationLawsTest.java) and [`OuterCorrelationDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/OuterCorrelationDiffTest.kt).
- Empty and malformed Number keys separate cleanly. Empty uses comparison-local zero at either origin; a malformed inner key drops only that candidate, while a malformed captured outer key drops every candidate whose comparison reads it. The checked neighboring non-law in [`Conformance/Correlation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/Correlation.lean) is that numeric `notEqual` is not Boolean negation of numeric `equal` on a malformed operand: both yield Lean `K.unknown` and keep neither row.
- Filtering precedes consumed-cell classification. A malformed consumed cell in a dropped row is not read; one in a kept row may make the consumer unknown. See [`SelfCorrelatedOverlapQuantifierDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/SelfCorrelatedOverlapQuantifierDiffTest.kt) and [`MultiStarFilteredCountDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/MultiStarFilteredCountDiffTest.kt).
- Validation and computation have different consequences inside the filter. Validation unknown drops a candidate because the filter is not true; computation reads contribute to dependency and poison behavior. See [`HavingOverComputedDependencyDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/HavingOverComputedDependencyDiffTest.kt).
- Polarity is not a simple consequence of selected values: filtered quantifiers/comparisons conservatively escalate, while filtered counts have an operand-dependent exception. See [`HavingQuantifierPolarityDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/HavingQuantifierPolarityDiffTest.kt), [`HavingStarComparisonPolarityDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/HavingStarComparisonPolarityDiffTest.kt), and [`FilteredCountPolarityDiffTest`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/FilteredCountPolarityDiffTest.kt).
- Evidence transport must preserve the raw stored condition because the documented kernel formatter defect can lose a `$`-bearing conjunct; see [`KERNEL-FINDINGS.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/KERNEL-FINDINGS.md). The one-time raw/compact migration therefore recorded and byte-checked every executed stored condition before accepting the normalized compact requests. Routine replay now pins those requests and the historical source digest; the raw stored conditions remain recoverable through the [validation archive](archived/VALIDATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md) rather than checked out beside them.

Lean consequence: represent selection as an ordered candidate-row filter with explicit inner and captured-outer origins, keep only known-true filters, and apply the selected consumer afterward. Checked lowering must establish group, scope, origin, kind, operator, and scale legality before constructing that core; an optimized join may be added only with a refinement proof against the straightforward selector. [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) owns the concrete current representation, [`IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md`](IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md) owns live support and proof status, and [`EVIDENCE.md`](EVIDENCE.md) owns the exact retained witnesses.

The first cross-level risk capsule makes the representation consequence executable. Its caller-established topology contains one starred parent level and one captured descendant; a filter requires the candidate parent row to equal both `CurrentRepetition($Parent)` and `CurrentRepetition($Parent/Child)`. Capture `{Parent = 1, Child = 1}` selects candidate `1`, while `{Parent = 1, Child = 2}` selects none. A scalar outer-row replacement changes the second result. Theorems establish exact named-level resolution and rule out one shared row when the captured coordinates differ. Missing or duplicate level bindings fail closed. The implementation remains `external evidence pending` because this repository retains no portable observation for the case, while the a12-dmkits reconciliation accepted under [`SPEC-2026-07-19-16`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-16---correlation-captures-every-named-outer-repetition-level) at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` now locks the diagonal/off-diagonal behavior through its maintained facilities.

Limit: the durable mechanism and raw two-level adapter do not imply checked nested paths, captured-row instantiation, multiple stars, joins, cross-group execution, general consumers, computation poison, or filtered-result polarity. They also do not expose the hidden kernel truth value behind an observed suppressed message. Those axes require separate capsules and evidence rather than expansion of this finding's current implementation narrative.

## LF4 — Cedar is the primary Lean engineering precedent; Radix is a scoped secondary check

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: cross-cutting. Basis: filesystem audit of Cedar specification revision `3977eb4f017b421b7ac0b31ea4635e1dd36ce3ef` and Radix revision `617b67eb09681ca98e19759b48978866dcafeb17`.

Cedar's actual source tree, not merely its project description, is the best default for this repository's Lean engineering shape. `Cedar/Spec` contains ordinary executable semantic definitions with explicit result/error domains and observable short-circuit order; `Cedar/Validation` is separate from execution; `Cedar/Thm` is a recursively aggregated theorem hierarchy; unit tests, symbolic tests, differential interfaces, CLI/FFI, and protobuf transport are separate libraries or executables. Its Lake linter recursively checks that the theorem root imports every proof file. The differential/FFI boundary may use `unsafe` and timing/IO, while the semantics and theorem layers remain pure. Its style guide favors minimal sorted imports, theorem docstrings, explicit types, `simp only` for stable rewrites, and explicit proof steps.

Lean treatment adopted here: keep `Semantics`, `Elaboration`, `Proofs`, and `Conformance` distinct; maintain one mechanically complete proof root; state residual non-success outcomes rather than claiming accepted input cannot fail; keep portable differential transport outside the trusted semantics; and inspect the complete elaborated trusted environment plus its dependency closure for trust escapes. This repository's explicit axiom and compiled-behavior audit is intentionally stronger than Cedar's import-completeness linter alone; LF11 records the mechanism.

The first iteration capsule applies Cedar's primary pattern concretely: the executable `SingleStar.select` stays in `Semantics`, the ordered `SelectRows` relation and exact two-way bridge stay in `Proofs`, and separating fixtures stay in `Conformance`. The `sumSelected_filter_before_consumer` theorem is the small reachability/noninterference result this slice can honestly support: after selection, consumer-cell agreement is required only on selected rows. This is preferable to inventing a full read-footprint framework before computation or partial validation needs one. Radix remains only a secondary confirmation that an evaluator/relation bridge is useful when the relation exposes independent structure.

Radix is useful when a compact technique needs confirmation. Its `BigStep` relation is genuinely independent from its fuel-based interpreter, and it proves fuel monotonicity plus soundness/completeness for successful terminating runs. Its optimization passes pair transformations with forward semantic-preservation theorems. Its top-level `Slides.lean` imports the real Radix library through Verso, so presentation examples elaborate against live code. These are valuable patterns only with their exact limits: the relation omits failure/divergence outcomes, the optimization results are forward preservation rather than unrestricted equivalence, and the slides dependency tracks an unpinned branch in the audited project.

Decision rule: inspect Cedar first for a new Lean architectural or proof-engineering choice. Use Radix to validate a small relational/evaluator/transformation or checked-exposition pattern when Cedar does not provide a compact example. Do not adopt either project's headline theorem counts, dependency choices, or theorem wording without reading the exact source, result domain, and hypotheses.

## LF5 — empty handling is a layered consuming-clause policy, not a field-kind function

> Date: 2026-07-13; amended 2026-07-15. Sections: §2, §3, §4, §6, §7, §8, §9, §11, §12. Basis: a12-dmkits' kernel-verified operator catalog and structural checks, focused differential tests, retained operator-sensitive capture, and read-only kernel source inspection.

The expert rule “empty input depends only on field type” is a useful baseline for direct comparisons, but `only` is false. The catalog has exactly eight kind defaults: Number is `AS_ZERO`, Confirm is `AS_FALSE`, and Boolean, String, Enumeration, Date, Time, and DateTime are `NOT_EVALUATED`. It also has 31 operators carrying empty-operand deviation blocks: 36 separate claims because five operators distinguish operand positions, backed by 39 executable probes. Every default and deviation claim has at least one probe. [`CATALOG-SPEC.md`](../../a12-rulekit/docs/CATALOG-SPEC.md#4-the-emptyoperand-dimension) defines this as a deviation-only layer and explicitly separates empty operands from invalid/not-check-relevant values.

The decisive same-kind counterexamples are independently visible in tests and source. An empty String suppresses a direct comparison, while `Length(empty String)` produces fillable zero and can fire `0 < 5`; the kernel source explicitly says that `Length` returns zero instead of unknown ([`EmptyValueLawsTest.java`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/EmptyValueLawsTest.java), [`BigDecimalUtils.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/BigDecimalUtils.java)). An empty Date suppresses direct comparison, while the date/time extractors and `DifferenceIn*` functions return fillable zero for an empty operand ([`EmptyOperandFiringDiffTest.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/EmptyOperandFiringDiffTest.kt), [`BedingungsOperatorHelper.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/BedingungsOperatorHelper.java)). Value-list membership, overlap predicates, and count/aggregate families further distinguish subject versus list-entry positions, `NOT_EVALUATED` versus `SKIPPED` versus `EVALUATES_FALSE`, and per-element treatment versus the identity of an all-empty selection.

Field kind and operator are still insufficient. An empty string-like result can suppress a direct comparison, contribute `""` inside concatenation, and produce `CLEARED` when stored as a standalone computation; [`FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/FieldValueAsStringComputeDiffTest.kt) pins the latter two contexts, while [`CalculationController.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CalculationController.java) rejects a no-value string at the computation store boundary. An index field is not another empty kind: its model role generates requiredness and uniqueness findings, so an empty or duplicate index cell becomes not-check-relevant before an aggregate or lookup consumes it ([`IndexAggregateDiffTest.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/IndexAggregateDiffTest.kt), [`IndexedReadSuppressionDiffTest.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/IndexedReadSuppressionDiffTest.kt)). Phase then controls whether findings expose validation unknown, computation poison, or—in the required-only carve-out—the underlying empty. A clean empty itself is not phase-normalized; it stays empty until a consumer interprets it. Row eligibility is an additional outer gate: the comparison defaults are probed in a content-bearing row because an entirely empty non-repeatable row may not be evaluated at all.

The evidence qualification matters. [`CatalogFacetProbesTest.java`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/CatalogFacetProbesTest.java) executes every catalog empty probe against the real kernel's Groovy-dynamic runtime and checks model acceptance, firing, and optional polarity. It does not run all 39 probes through the static-Java strategy. Separate differential tests tri-check both strategies for selected high-risk mechanisms, so the defensible claim is complete dynamic-runtime probe coverage of the catalog claims, not universal two-strategy coverage of every catalog cell.

Lean treatment: keep `CellObservation.empty` unresolved at the shared read boundary. Never expose a universal `FieldKind → Value` empty normalization and never make `Phase` an empty-substitution table. Each operator family owns named resolution clauses for the relevant operand positions and preserves the provenance its consumer observes: a symmetric clause may need given/substituted, while numeric polarity needs independent grow/shrink directions. Functions may turn empty into a concrete derived value such as fillable zero; otherwise no-value remains explicit so a comparison, concatenation, or store can make its own decision. Aggregates specify two independent clauses: per-element empty treatment and all-empty selection identity. Model roles add staged findings during elaboration/document passes rather than mutating the scalar kind policy.

The current design now demonstrates this boundary rather than merely preparing for it. [`Observation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/Observation.lean) keeps absent and present-empty as different checked placements, including normalization of a parsed empty String to present-empty, while projecting both to `.empty`; [`formalCheck_empty_observes_empty`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/Observation.lean) and the generic present-empty laws lock non-normalization at the phase read. [`FlatValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/FlatValidation.lean) then gives direct String equality/inequality, presence, and `Length` distinct consumers: both comparisons are not evaluated on an empty field or literal, presence sees no evaluation value, and Length becomes grow-only zero. Malformed field input is classified before empty-literal suppression. [`StringLength.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/StringLength.lean) proves the shared empty-comparison laws, all four reduced ordering polarities, same-field Length distinction, and placement/presence non-law. Direct Number comparison delegates to directional fillability rather than treating every substituted firing as omission; LF10 owns that refinement, while LF61 owns the placement/value distinction. `Length ==`/`!=` remain rejected only by the scale-erasing flat surface; the complete numeric atom retains authored scale and admits them together with arithmetic, wrappers, computation, and generated validation. The rest of the deviation matrix remains open. Each future deviation still needs a paired witness, a checked false generalization, and retained kernel observation.

Limit: the catalog deliberately leaves some construction and conversion families uncharacterized. The 31-operator inventory is therefore a verified snapshot of recorded deviations for kernel 30.8.1, not a proof that every other operator follows the kind default. Unknown matrix cells remain open evidence obligations rather than inferred defaults.

## LF6 — bare-name resolution is local-or-global, not an ancestor walk

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: §10. Basis: kernel parser source, two new kernel-confirmed rejection probes, retained positive local/model-wide cases, and a12-dmkits parser treatment.

The earlier three-tier account—declaring group, then nearest ancestors, then model-wide unique fallback—was falsified. It came from an earlier revision of [`spec/08-paths-and-references.md`](../spec/08-paths-and-references.md) and made the first Lean elaborator internally consistent but externally wrong. The original positive “bare ancestor” witness was underdetermined: its field name was globally unique, so both an ancestor walk and model-wide fallback selected the same field.

The kernel mechanism has two tiers. [`CheckIdNameRefImpl.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/entity/CheckIdNameRefImpl.java) first constructs exactly `declaringGroup/name` and accepts that entity if present. On a miss, a non-absolute reference may use the flag-gated model-wide short-name index: one match resolves, multiple matches raise `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`, and no match raises `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`. It never walks ancestor groups. The a12-dmkits parser documents and implements the same local-candidate-then-global rule in [`Parser.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/parse/Parser.kt).

The separating evidence holds all other relevant structure fixed. A bare `Limit` in a nested group with `Limit` at two ancestor levels is rejected as `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`, instead of choosing the nearest. A second model with one unique ancestor but short-name fallback disabled is rejected as `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`, instead of walking upward. Conversely, a same-named declaring-group field wins even when an ancestor has that name, and a unique cross-group name resolves when the flag is enabled; both positive cases are retained runtime observations. The current records live in the [compact validation bundle](../evidence/kernel-30.8.1/captures/validation-core-v1/semantic-observations.json), and their complete historical diagnostics remain recoverable through the [validation archive](archived/VALIDATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md).

Lean treatment: [`FlatModel.resolveBare`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/Flat.lean) now checks only `declaringGroup/name` before the flag-gated model-wide uniqueness query. The unused ancestor-search helpers were removed. [`Conformance/Elaboration.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/Elaboration.lean) locks the local win, globally unique fallback, duplicate-name rejection, and flag-disabled rejection. Explicit parent-relative resolution remains unchanged and is covered by matching and mismatching retained observations.

This finding overrides LF2's original path-order wording. The corrected two-tier rule is now canonical in [`spec/08-paths-and-references.md` §2](../spec/08-paths-and-references.md#2-bare-name-resolution-order-); kernel 30.8.1 remains authoritative, while this finding retains the falsification and Lean-treatment history.

## LF7 — a malformed `Having` filter drops its row before consumption

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: §3, §9. Basis: the retained [compact validation bundle](../evidence/kernel-30.8.1/captures/validation-core-v1/semantic-observations.json) — the read-order discriminator is the neighbouring `having-malformed-consumer-dropped`/`-kept` pair, in which the malformed cell is the *consumer*; the `having-malformed-filter-drops` record puts the malformed cell in the *filter* and therefore does not separate filter-before-consumer from the alternative, a distinction this basis line previously blurred — direct agreement of kernel 30.8.1 Groovy-dynamic and static-Java strategies, and the a12-dmkits triangulation split preserved in the [raw-evidence archive](archived/VALIDATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md).

For rows `(Weight, Count) = (malformed, 100), (1, 3), (1, 4)`, the rule `Sum(Items*/Count Having Weight == 1) == 7` fires. The malformed Weight still produces its formal-error message, but its row-local filter comparison is not true and the row is dropped before Count is consumed; the remaining selected counts sum to seven. Both kernel strategies produce the authored OMISSION message and the formal error.

The historical a12-dmkits interpreter reported only the formal error for this witness. The exact external split and recovery identity are preserved in the [raw-evidence archive](archived/VALIDATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md), while routine replay retains the kernel-authored outcome in the compact bundle. This does not weaken the Lean evidence: by LF1 the interpreter is a peer for triangulation, not the oracle, and the two agreeing kernel strategies determine the observation. It is recorded explicitly rather than hidden, dropped from the capsule, or encoded as a kernel-strategy divergence in the compact case.

Lean treatment: `SingleStar.select` evaluates the filter first and `Verdict.keepsHaving` keeps only `.fired`; validation `.unknown` therefore drops the row. `sumSelected_filter_before_consumer` proves the broader internal boundary that dropped-row consumer cells are irrelevant once selection agrees. The neighboring retained pair changes only the malformed consumer row's Weight from `2` to `1`: dropped means the authored rule fires, kept means the sum is unknown and the authored rule is silent.

Limit: the retained external message boundary establishes firing versus silence and the observed OMISSION signature, not the kernel's hidden internal representation of the malformed filter as Lean `Verdict.unknown`. This finding does not diagnose or fix the a12-dmkits interpreter's internal cause, and this repository must not modify that sibling project.

## LF8 — reusable Lean domains and kernel-backed public domains are different boundaries

> Date: 2026-07-13. Sections: §5, §9, cross-cutting protocol/evidence. Basis: cold-handover audit of the checked correlation API, all 12 retained runtime correlation projections, and the first language-neutral candidate suite.

The reusable correlation core deliberately accepts any positive duplicate-free candidate IDs and scans them in list order. That is a useful total semantic abstraction, but it is not harmless generality at the public kernel-correspondence boundary: `CurrentRepetition` compares the numeric `RowIndex` itself, while selection order comes separately from the list. Gapped or reordered IDs can therefore change comparison truth, not merely rename rows. Every retained kernel correlation observation uses actual contiguous one-based repetition indices `[1..n]`; no retained case establishes arbitrary positive IDs as kernel documents.

The same distinction appears in Number transport. The Lean account resolves exact rational values and applies scale-19 `HALF_UP` comparison, and the normalized decoder accepts canonical exact decimals. The runtime correlation projection schema transports integers, and the retained cases exercise only non-negative integer cells. Static evidence establishes selected declared-scale legality, but it does not establish dynamic negative, fractional, or scale-19 rounding behavior inside correlation.

Lean treatment: keep the reusable semantic and checked-elaboration domains general where that aids proofs and later evidence, but narrow the normalized `singleGroupCorrelation.firingRows` operation to non-empty contiguous candidate sequences. Machine-readable support metadata and the implementer kit state that general exact-decimal and signed correlation inputs are Lean-account behavior with external evidence pending. The handover methodology has two conceptual lanes: the finite candidate suite of retained cases and broader Lean differential/property generation outside that suite; it must never relabel the second lane as new external evidence.

This is not an argument to weaken internal types until they match today’s corpus. It is a rule for public support claims: a representation generalization becomes a released kernel-semantic capability only after its observable consequences are researched and retained. A future probe may close negative/fractional values or show that a different document adapter supplies row identity; until then, downstream implementers receive an exact answer and an exact claim class rather than being asked to repeat kernel research.

## LF9 — the bounded process contract depends on pinned Lean runtime details

> Date: 2026-07-15. Sections: cross-cutting process and differential testing. Basis: the pinned Lean 4.31 process API documentation and native runtime implementation, [`A12Kernel/Process/Bounded.lean`](../A12Kernel/Process/Bounded.lean), [`RelayMain.lean`](../A12Kernel/Process/RelayMain.lean), and the adversarial [`ProcessTestMain.lean`](../A12Kernel/ProcessTestMain.lean) gate.

Lean 4.31 documents that `IO.Process.Child.kill` terminates the whole POSIX process group when the child was spawned with `setsid := true`. The pinned native implementation makes that behavior depend on metadata stored in the Lean child handle. Its `Child.takeStdin` path returns a replacement handle without preserving the `setsid` bit, even though the operating-system process remains its session leader. A parent that spawns a candidate with piped stdin, calls `takeStdin`, and later calls `kill` can therefore kill only the candidate PID while leaving descendants alive. The public API shape alone does not reveal this liveness failure.

Lean treatment: the bounded parent starts a project-owned relay with null stdin, piped output, and `setsid := true`, so it retains the original child handle and its group-kill metadata. A capped request is placed in an automatically removed temporary file; the relay starts the candidate in the same process group, feeds its stdin, and streams candidate stdout and stderr without buffering them itself. Only after candidate stdin, exit, and both output streams complete does the relay overwrite that file with a strict completion record containing a magic value and canonical decimal candidate exit code. The parent bounds and parses that record independently, so candidate status and diagnostics cannot be mistaken for relay status.

Candidate input is a best-effort delivery channel once the candidate closes its read end. A fast candidate may legitimately produce a result and exit without consuming stdin; on Linux this can race the relay's writer and surface `EPIPE` as Lean's `IO.Error.resourceVanished`. Treating that closed-reader signal as a relay failure made binary-transparent execution platform-schedule-dependent. The relay now ignores only `resourceVanished` from its candidate-stdin writer, still propagates every other input error, and emits a prefixed diagnostic for its own failures instead of collapsing them to an unexplained exit code. The adversarial gate reproduces the mechanism with a full-size ignored request independently of the original fast binary-output case.

Cleanup order is a safety invariant, not an implementation detail. Starting `child.wait` too early can reap the relay leader before the process-group kill; the operating system may then reuse its PID as another process-group ID, making a later group kill capable of targeting an unrelated group. The bounded parent instead treats EOF on both relay output pipes as the completion signal, kills the owned group while the unreaped relay leader still reserves its PID, and only then starts the bounded wait. It applies the same kill-before-wait sequence on timeout, stream failure, output overflow, and success, which also prevents a same-group descendant that closed its inherited streams from surviving an otherwise successful invocation.

The result is a finite resource boundary, not hostile-code containment. Input, stdout, stderr, execution, cleanup, poll, aggregate campaign, and result limits are explicit and checked, but the candidate runs with inherited credentials, working directory, and environment. A deliberately hostile child can inspect or modify anything available to that user, create a new session to escape the process group, or otherwise attack the host. The supported contract is cooperative POSIX execution on macOS and Linux; it is not a Windows contract and must not be described as a sandbox.

Toolchain consequence: every change to [`lean-toolchain`](../lean-toolchain), and every proposed extension beyond the declared macOS/Linux profile, must re-audit the process API and native `spawn`, `takeStdin`, `kill`, and wait implementation before the bounded-runner claim is reused. Then rerun `lake exe checkBoundedProcess` on every supported platform, including timeout, blocked and deliberately ignored input, exact and overflowing outputs, invalid UTF-8, relay diagnostics, nonzero status separation, and both success- and failure-path descendant cleanup. A compatible type signature or successful ordinary child execution is not enough to preserve this contract.

## LF10 — numeric polarity needs directional fillability, not a given bit

> Date: 2026-07-15. Sections: §2, §5, §7, §12. Basis: [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md), read-only kernel and a12-dmkits polarity mechanisms, the retained six-case operator-sensitive capture, red/green conformance, and trusted directional-polarity proofs.

The earlier flat evaluator attached only `given : Bool` to a comparison operand and classified every true comparison over a substituted value as OMISSION. That loses the reason polarity exists: OMISSION means filling could clear the currently true error condition, so the evaluator must know which directions filling can move the value. The bug is visible while holding the substituted value and operator fixed. An empty unsigned Number reads as zero and can grow but cannot shrink: `0 != -1` is therefore VALUE because filling cannot reach `-1`, whereas `0 != 1` is OMISSION because filling could reach `1`. An empty signed Number can move in either direction, so its `0 != -1` is OMISSION. A filled zero is fixed and makes each true inequality VALUE.

The String deviation exposes the same directional mechanism after a different consumer. Direct equality over an empty String is not evaluated, but `Length(empty)` is numeric zero whose future value can only grow. Consequently `Length(empty) < 5` fires as OMISSION, while `Length(empty) >= 0` fires as VALUE. The six retained cases hold the models and three-rule inventories fixed across empty, filled, and empty-row documents; both kernel strategies and a12-dmkits agree on the complete external message lists. This establishes the named observable outcomes, not every internal branch or every String and Number operator.

Lean treatment: [`A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericFillability.lean) owns `NumericFillability {canGrow, canShrink}` separately from exact `Rat` amounts. [`A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericComparison.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericComparison.lean) owns shared scale-19 normalization and computes whether movement on either operand can falsify a firing; `evalFixedRight` specializes the general evaluator with a fixed literal for reduced consumers. Filled scalars use `fixed`; empty unsigned Number and empty `Length` use `growOnly`; empty signed Number uses both directions. `NumericComparisonOp` contains all six direct ordinary operators, while the reduced checked String-Length surface admits exactly the four scale-exempt ordering operators because it does not retain a literal's authored scale. The theorem [`growOnlyNotEqualWhenLeftNotBelow_is_value`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericComparison.lean) states the reusable fixed-right law; conformance locks both `-1` and the predicted second instance `-2`, so a special-case patch for one literal would fail.

This finding corrects the universal one-bit Lean modelling note in [`spec/03-empty-and-required.md`](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md). A one-bit not-given flag can serve a simple symmetric consumer—Confirm's substituted false is the current example—but it is not the language-wide provenance account: numeric expressions and numeric aggregates need directional fillability, counts are grow-only, dates and strings use their separate not-given rules, and filters retain explicit escalation clauses in [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md). The public flat operation already admitted Number inequality, so the semantic correction changes the result of an accepted request such as empty unsigned Number `!= -1`; no public String/Length surface was added. The current account is 0.3.0 with V2 suites. The superseded 0.2.0 behavior, its OMISSION-versus-VALUE separating result, and the completed Rust capability are preserved only in the [archived historical record](archived/REFERENCE-SEMANTICS-0.2.0-AND-RUST-EXPERIMENT.md), not as current compatibility machinery.

Limit at the time of this finding: the first polarity helper had one fillable left expression and a fixed literal. LF28 and LF29 subsequently closed valid-operand arithmetic propagation, LF52 closes the resolved pure power branch table, and LF34 closes the plain two-expression ordinary/tolerance route including power. Aggregate fillability, date symmetry, concatenation flags, filtered polarity, partial validation, and the UTF-16 astral boundary externally remain separate consuming-clause and evidence obligations.

## LF11 — trust boundaries must inspect Lean's elaborated environment, not source spellings

> Date: 2026-07-15. Sections: cross-cutting proof and executable trust. Basis: adversarial Lean 4.31 declaration probes, the audited Cedar separation between specification/theorems and compiler/FFI integration, and the project trust-gate review.

A line-oriented ban on `axiom`, `unsafe`, `partial`, or selected attributes is useful only as defense in depth. Lean permits declaration modifiers, same-line and multiline attributes, late `attribute` commands, and command macros. A macro can therefore expand an innocent-looking custom command into an axiom, while `implemented_by` can give compiled execution a different body from the kernel-visible definition and `extern` can route it to foreign code. A theorem-root `#print axioms` list also cannot expose an unused project axiom. These are mechanism-level gaps, not reasons to add another list of spelling exceptions.

Lean treatment: [`A12Kernel/Trust/Environment.lean`](../A12Kernel/Trust/Environment.lean) audits the `ConstantInfo` values owned by every imported trusted project module. It rejects any project `axiomInfo`, `isUnsafe` declaration, unclassified `opaqueInfo`, non-generated partial declaration, `implemented_by` mapping, `extern` entry, or dependency outside `propext`, `Classical.choice`, and `Quot.sound`. The opaque rule deliberately rejects source `partial` wrappers and bodyless inhabited stubs; a future legitimate opaque abstraction needs an explicit policy decision rather than passing accidentally. Lean also creates partial `_unsafe_rec` compiler helpers for total recursive definitions, so the audit admits that exact shape only when its parent is a kernel-visible safe `defnInfo` with the identical type. Treating every `isPartial` constant as authored `partial` would reject ordinary total recursion and would misunderstand Lean's compiled representation; accepting the suffix alone would let a forged helper borrow an unsafe or unrelated parent.

[`scripts/check-lean-trust.sh`](../scripts/check-lean-trust.sh) verifies proof-root imports, the named human-readable theorem registry, and positive source-zone closure. Every current project Lean source is classified; the logical closure admits only logical sources, conformance admits logical plus conformance, and the library root admits only its root plus those two zones. Evidence, binding, registry, reference, process, trust, and IO drivers therefore cannot enter merely because a future path was missing from a blacklist. [`A12Kernel/Trust/Adversarial.lean`](../A12Kernel/Trust/Adversarial.lean) elaborates attributed and macro-generated axioms, unsafe and partial declarations, late compiler/foreign attributes, `sorry`, `native_decide`, and opaque stubs in one nonlogical session, then requires the environment audit to reject each for the predicted reason. An accepted control contains those words only as String data and uses an ordinary non-substituting attribute plus the reviewed logical axioms. Conformance remains outside the logical environment audit because its explicit `native_decide` examples are regression locks whose compiler/runtime trust is already classified.

Limit: this audit establishes the declared Lean project trust policy for the pinned toolchain; it is not a proof of Lean itself or of native compiler correctness. Toolchain upgrades must revalidate the environment APIs, generated-recursion classification, and adversarial probes. Source scanning remains deliberately non-authoritative, and the trust driver itself stays outside the trusted roots it inspects.

## LF12 — String computation has expression, store, and delta boundaries

> Date: 2026-07-15. Sections: §2, §7, §11. Basis: [`spec/09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md), read-only kernel and a12-dmkits computation mechanisms, the retained [compact root-String projection](../evidence/kernel-30.8.1/captures/string-computation-v1/semantic-observations.json) and its [raw recovery record](archived/STRING-COMPUTATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md), red/green conformance, and trusted evaluator/store/delta proofs.

An empty String does not have one context-free computation result. A bare field copy reads as clean no-value. Concatenation consumes the same missing operand as the empty contribution `""`, so an empty source plus `"-X"` evaluates and stores `"-X"`. But the fact that concatenation evaluated does not make an all-empty final text storable: `[Source] + [Other]` over two empty fields reaches evaluated text `""`, then the root String store treats that final empty text as no calculation. A stale target is therefore `CLEARED`; an absent target remains silent. This expression-versus-root distinction is now canonical in [`spec/09-computations.md` §2](../spec/09-computations.md#2-separate-operations-and-the-three-outcomes); the finding retains its evidence and Lean consequence rather than acting as a correction to that clause.

The 13 retained cases separate direct copy, suffix concatenation, and two-field concatenation while varying filled versus empty source, stale versus absent target, and content-bearing versus independently empty row. Groovy-dynamic and static-Java kernel strategies agree on every complete target-delta list. At the retained 699e-era revision the a12-dmkits interpreter agrees in ten cases but reports `VALUE|` for the three all-empty variants where the kernel clears or stays silent. That exact raw result is mechanically projected to kernel delta granularity and retained as triangulation, not hidden or used as the oracle. The mismatch identifies one reusable implementation rule: final computed empty String must be rejected at the root store boundary even though empty remains a legal intermediate concatenation contribution. a12-dmkits later confirmed and fixed that rule as IF123; the correction strengthens current triangulation but never rewrites the historical capture.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/StringComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/StringComputation.lean) separates `StringTerm` expression results, `StringStore` root decisions, and `StringDelta` reporting against a typed prior target. `StringExpr` is parameterized only by its leaf, allowing [`Elaboration/StringComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/StringComputation.lean) to resolve structured paths into the same runtime tree and return a wrapper certifying model validity plus nonrepeatable String identity for every field ID. Its public evaluation method checks raw cells through that same model before delegation, so a caller cannot pair the proof with another model's checked context. No second expression evaluator is introduced. `StoredString` makes an empty stored target unrepresentable. Computation-phase field reads preserve clean absence versus poison, concatenation is left-to-right and short-circuits after a left poison, while an empty left contribution still reads a poisoned right operand; root storage alone collapses evaluated `text ""` to no-value. The trusted proof spine connects `CheckedCell` observation to the library evaluator, proves literal and concatenation lowering preservation, exact model-bound evaluator delegation, empty contribution on both sides, term-level associativity with poison order, store and delta preservation under a missing contribution, final-empty rejection, typed change reporting, and the nearest stronger non-law: missing is not an identity at the intermediate term layer. This makes root-store or delta preservation—not raw expression equality—the safe boundary for a String refactoring. Term-level associativity alone does not authorize reassociating `StringExpr` trees across effectful reads; that future transformation needs an evaluator-level preservation theorem with explicit context assumptions.

Limit: the external record contains only unconditional, non-repeatable, clean String inputs and unconstrained String targets. It directly establishes final `VALUE`/`CLEARED`/silence deltas, not Lean's intermediate term/store states. Poison and its immediate clear projection are source-grounded internal account behavior but are not distinguished by this record. Checked lowering covers decoded copy/literal/concatenation expressions and, through LF13's later closure, one ordinary nonrepeatable target with its declaration-owned line-break/min/max policy and direct self-reference rejection. Whole computation tables, pattern/custom/Enumeration/repeatable targets, concrete syntax, date-shaped literal classification, dependency graphs and scheduling, implicit validation, externally calibrated non-ASCII target behavior, and general concatenation formatting remain separate capsules. LF18 adds exact one-address application with partial external calibration.

## LF13 — String target outcome, delta, and application are separate boundaries

> Date: 2026-07-15. Sections: §3, §7, §11. Basis: [`spec/09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md), read-only kernel and a12-dmkits computed-result mechanisms, the retained nine-case [compact root-String projection](../evidence/kernel-30.8.1/captures/string-computation-v1/semantic-observations.json), its [raw recovery and agreement record](archived/STRING-COMPUTATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md), red/green conformance, and trusted target-check/delta/application proofs.

A nonempty root String write attempt is not yet a successful computation result. The target's reduced computed-result check classifies that attempt as accepted or errored before reporting compares it with the prior target. An accepted value equal to the prior typed value remains a clean accepted outcome but produces no change delta. A target error is different from both clean no-value and computation poison: it retains the attempted stored String and a cause, and `ERRORED` is reported unconditionally whether the prior target was absent, stale, or already equal to the invalid attempt. Equality suppression therefore belongs only to accepted `VALUE`, never to `ERRORED`.

The archived nine-case capture holds the copy expression and target address fixed across positive `maxLength` 4 versus 3 models, with absent, stale, and equal prior-target cases; adds exact positive `minLength` 5 boundary and short controls; and adds a padded `" " + [Source] + " "` result accepted exactly at `maxLength` 5. Groovy-dynamic and Java-static kernel strategies agree on all nine complete rich computation results, change-only deltas, and exact applied-target observations. The a12-dmkits interpreter agrees on all nine projected deltas and stored-value application views. The equal accepted case is still present in the kernel's clean-result collection with `changed = false`, while the equal invalid case remains payloadful `ERRORED`; the padded control establishes for this admitted ASCII result that leading and trailing blanks survive the reduced target check rather than receiving the baseline input-field blank rejection. a12-dmkits remains triangulation, not evidence authority. F11 records the old compact-signature gap; the historical `compute-observation-v1` boundary transported rich per-runner observations with explicit unavailable fidelity instead of pretending the compact route was lossless. That producer estate was later retired without changing the retained observations.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/StringComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/StringComputation.lean) inserts `StringTargetOutcome` after `StringStore` and before delta projection. The ordinary target no longer has a parallel length-only representation: `StringFieldPolicy.checkTarget` reuses the declaration-owned line-break/minimum/maximum checker, including inert zero bounds, combined bounds, and permitted CRLF normalization for UTF-16 measurement. The target boundary deliberately ignores the checker's normalized success payload and retains the exact attempted `StoredString` for both `accepted` and `errored`; forbidden CR/LF is the payloadful `.lineBreak` error. [`Elaboration/StringComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/StringComputation.lean) binds that policy to the actual nonrepeatable ordinary String declaration and excludes direct target self-reference, raw targets, and registered-custom targets. `StringTargetOutcome.errored` and `StringDelta.errored` retain the attempt and first cause, accepted results keep the change-sensitive delta rule, errors bypass prior-target equality, and `appliedValue` remains deliberately lossy. The proof spine now states exact attempt preservation on both target-check results, line-break precedence, no-value/poison bypass, declaration admission, self-reference exclusion, shared-evaluator delegation, unconditional error reporting, and the nearest stronger non-law: equal value-only application results do not imply equal deltas because no-value can be silent where `ERRORED` remains visible. LF18 retains the separate exact-state transition rather than changing this projection.

Limit: the retained kernel-correspondence record remains a positive, one-bound-only String-length capsule over nonempty conservative ASCII values. Combined/zero bounds, permitted CRLF and lone-CR target attempts, forbidden-break errors, no-value/poison bypass, and integrated target lowering are source-grounded and internally locked but not exercised by that packet. Pattern, Enumeration, raw, registered-custom, repeatable, and other target kinds remain outside the ordinary checked operation, as do initially present-empty packet cases, general computation ordering/scheduling, whole-document application, implicit validation integration, and public protocol exposure.

## LF14 — CRLF normalization is an ingestion boundary, not merely a target-length helper

> Date: 2026-07-17. Sections: §2, §7, §11. Basis: [`spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md` §B.3](../spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md#b3-what-puts-a-cell-in-the-third-state), [`spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md` Part A](../spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md#part-a--7-strings-and-patterns), [`LEAN-ENCODING-GUIDE.md` §§2–3](LEAN-ENCODING-GUIDE.md), the a12-dmkits IF124 dual-route probes and IF125 review discriminator, and the current fail-closed Lean String target boundary. The behavior is canonical in `spec/`; no local retained CR/LF packet yet establishes Lean correspondence.

Kernel 30.8.1 normalizes CRLF to LF when an input value enters evaluation, before the normalized value populates the evaluation caches. The effect therefore reaches every later consumer of that evaluated String, including computations and length checks; it is not a local cleanup performed only by one target validator. Lone CR remains unnormalized. The same investigation fixed a distinct clause-order issue in which line-break handling precedes enumeration-domain validation. A separate IF125 discriminator found that a raw-type `Length(nvv) > c` rule can be eliminated during code generation rather than evaluated with an ordinary empty or normalized operand. These are different mechanisms and must not be collapsed into one “String length” branch.

Lean treatment: [`normalizeEvaluatedString`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/String.lean) performs one non-overlapping CRLF-to-LF pass when declaration-owned checking constructs an evaluated String. Both phases, direct comparison, UTF-16 `Length`, and computation reads consume that cached result. Existing LF and lone CR are preserved. The checked counterexample `"\r\r\n"` is essential: one pass yields `"\r\n"` and a second pass would yield `"\n"`, so global idempotence is false and no consumer may normalize independently. This ingestion capsule remains `external evidence pending`; no retained portable packet currently exercises its complete CRLF/LF/lone-CR matrix. The direct comparison surface has since widened beyond the original field-to-literal boundary, but any future external matrix must still exercise the exact claimed legal shape rather than silently project another one. Ordinary computed targets now reuse the same policy checker: target permission is declaration-owned, permitted text is normalized only in a temporary checking view, and the exact attempted payload is retained. Enumeration ordering, pattern/custom target execution, and wider exact stored-document behavior remain separate rather than inferred from ingestion alone.

Limit: IF124 and IF125 are confirmed and guarded in current a12-dmkits, but that project remains knowledge, corpus/differential infrastructure, and clean-room triangulation rather than the oracle. Until their complete kernel observations are retained and replayed here through a fit-for-purpose accepted route, they justify the fail-closed design and future scenario matrix, not a new Lean correspondence claim or expansion of the supported String domain.

## LF15 — A dependent computation read has two failure domains, and requiredness is not ordinary poison

> Date: 2026-07-17. Sections: §3, §11. Basis: the direct String-cascade red/green proof study under Lean 4.31.0, the existing phase-indexed observation laws, and the accepted five-case cascade packet's public separating behavior.

Mapping a dependent `readTerm` over `withDependencyOutcome` intentionally produces a nested result. The outer `Except StringDependencyFault` says whether the producer outcome can form a computation dependency; the inner `Except StringComputationFault` says whether the later field read belongs to the admitted String fragment. Flattening the pair, or proving only the successful inner projection, would hide a real boundary.

The later SG4 source audit corrected what that first proof study over-modeled. A synthetic cell with one ordinary finding observes as computation poison, but the kernel does not retain that finding when the cell is a computed target: its cache stores only invalid-target membership and creates the same cascading exception for every reached invalid computed value or presence read. Lean therefore maps every reachable invalid producer—target rejection or inherited poison—to one transient `.computedDependency` marker, erasing attempted payload, target-error subclass, and originating formal cause. Clean no-value stays empty and therefore distinct. A directly forged `.poison .required` still fails at the outer boundary because computation observation ignores validation-scoped requiredness and no checked computation can supply it as an ordinary reached poison.

Lean treatment: [`FormalCause.computedDependency`](../A12Kernel/Cell.lean) is not a document formal error or message; it is the checked-cell-compatible transient marker for the cause-blind computation overlay. [`Proofs/Observation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/Observation.lean) owns the phase-level clean-value and non-required-poison equations. [`Proofs/StringCascade.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/StringCascade.lean) proves that distinct reachable invalid producer outcomes induce the same dependency read. The nontrusted [`StringCascadeProjection.lean`](../A12Kernel/Evidence/StringCascadeProjection.lean) still decodes the five producer-certified compact cases at their public clean/error/delta/value-only application boundary, which never exposed the former internal cause claim.

Limit: the nested-failure statement remains an internal law of the candidate two-step Lean account. The cause erasure itself is source-determined by the invalid-set and generated-catch mechanism; the retained compact bundle supplies replayed correspondence for five named public outcomes but does not directly expose the hidden dependency cell or either internal fault tag. LF18 separately models exact one-address application; it does not turn the compact cascade projection into exact-state correspondence. General repeatable scheduling, validation-to-computation inheritance beyond `.required`, and unread poison remain outside the cascade capsule.

## LF16 — certify transport once, then compare typed semantic observations

> Date: 2026-07-17. Sections: cross-cutting evidence architecture. Basis: the archived [direct-cascade raw unit](archived/STRING-DIRECT-CASCADE-RAW-EVIDENCE.md), the accepted [`compact semantic-evidence plan`](SEMANTIC-CAPSULE-PIPELINE-PROPOSAL.md), and the red/green `ObservationBundle` and `StringCascadeProjection` consumer study.

The first complete packet proved that the upstream producer could establish its receipts, capabilities, runner fidelity, qualification, closure, and cross-artifact relations, but also showed that reconstructing them in every Lean family is not scalable. The replacement gave ordinary Lean replay one bounded operation-neutral bundle plus a typed family decoder; the accepted raw unit then moved to a named Git recovery revision. The producer checks remain pinned historical assurance recoverable at their a12-dmkits revisions; current upstream `main` later retired the unused implementation.

The completed direct-cascade consumer demonstrates the useful minimum. Its operation-neutral reader and tests total 367 nonblank lines; its family projection is 249 and reuses `StoredString`, `PriorStringTarget`, and `StringTargetError`, while its family tests total 130. The family pins a12-dmkits exporter revision `1b5f463b89adc6cfb81b41121cd6c97855e8cbe3`, compact-bundle SHA-256 `1d8d253e553eba70fa990975666884833748bed9d9b2b6483f472767a9837c7a`, and the two retained receipt identities before it decodes the five inputs, preserves clean/change/error/clear/applied lists with multiplicity in canonical producer-before-consumer order, and compares the real evaluator result directly. It does not parse or hash the raw packet.

The producer-certified compact lane and complete legacy binder agreed once on all five cases. The migration deleted the legacy binder, operation schema/replay/tests, and receipt decoder/tests. Its first scoped comparison measured 666 against a 2,179-line packet-specific target, but that checkpoint excluded 359 nonblank lines of shared artifact/tree production support. The all-in consolidation deleted those helpers and their tests, moved only the live guards into the reader, and settled the complete reader, tests, family, tests, and seven driver lines at 753 nonblank lines—272 fewer than the honest 1,025-line all-in checkpoint—with no permanent dual path. The later raw archive migration removed another 31 files and 123,184 bytes from `HEAD` while retaining the compact bundle and exact recovery identities. A real mutation that bypassed the producer-outcome overlay failed the compact family gate and was restored before the natural replay returned green. Removing the overlay, changing the observed error cause, reversing an observation, or duplicating one therefore remains observable at the small typed boundary.

The historical a12-dmkits one-family exporter itself exceeded its provisional 550-line estimate and was accepted at 778 nonblank production/test/task lines after three independent audits established that the excess was explicit closed projection checks and diagnostics rather than a second capture framework. It was subsequently retired with the rest of portable capture V1. That exception is neither a budget template nor shared logic waiting to be extracted: a future family starts from its concrete observation need, reuses a current corpus/differential route when it fits, or requests the smallest new producer capability. Receipt, runner, qualification, filesystem, and closure machinery must not reappear per family or be resurrected merely because the old framework once existed.

## LF17 — dead error conditions and uninhabitable models are different queries

> Date: 2026-07-18. Sections: §3, §5, §11, §12, cross-cutting analysis. Basis: [`spec/01-data-model.md`](../spec/01-data-model.md), [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md), the current `Verdict` and phase-sensitive formal-observation semantics, and the corrected a12-dmkits [G40/SME-TDG contract](../../a12-rulekit/docs/FEATURE-GAPS.md#3-open) at revision `93e2bd57fc53db49cd36b743d3912e2dc5088df2`.

An authored A12 rule describes an error condition. Therefore an internally contradictory condition such as `[A] > 5 And [A] < 3` is unreachable and never fires; it does not by itself make the model uninhabitable. Whole-model inhabitance instead asks whether some structurally and formally admissible document survives generated checks, computation/application under a fixed profile, and every invalidating authored rule. Multiple individually reachable error rules can exhaust the admissible domain even though no rule condition is internally contradictory.

The distinction cannot be repaired by Boolean polarity alone. A malformed cell can observe as validation `unknown`, so merely asserting that every authored rule is not `fired` would let a solver manufacture false samples through formal invalidity. Repetition shape, requiredness and index checks, computation order, severity, suppression, and the fixed world are also part of the accepted-document relation. Computation-alternative overlap and gap are separate existential queries again.

Lean treatment: keep the relations separately named; illustrative future names include `conditionTruthReachable`, `ruleFiringReachable`, `modelInhabited`, `alternativeOverlap`, and `alternativeGap`. Any analyzer consumes only a checked supported model and states exact bounds and profile inputs. A synthesized SAT witness supports a semantic claim only after it is checked against the exact source relation. Solver UNSAT supports whole-model uninhabitance only when source-to-constraint completeness, constraint rendering, and solver trust or a checked certificate are all established for the stated fragment and bounds; otherwise it remains solver-reported or bounded.

Limit: this finding fixes the analysis vocabulary and proof obligations. It does not define an accepted-document relation, establish an exact SME TDG acceptance profile, adopt a solver capability, or imply an implementation sequence. Current coverage and open adequacy obligations remain owned by [`IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md`](IMPLEMENTATION-MAP.md).

## LF18 — exact target placement is separate from value and delta

> Date: 2026-07-18. Sections: §1, §11. Basis: [`spec/01-data-model.md`](../spec/01-data-model.md), [`spec/09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md), the retained nine-case [compact root-String projection](../evidence/kernel-30.8.1/captures/string-computation-v1/semantic-observations.json) and its [raw recovery record](archived/STRING-COMPUTATION-RAW-EVIDENCE.md), the archived [direct-cascade raw unit](archived/STRING-DIRECT-CASCADE-RAW-EVIDENCE.md), red/green exact-state conformance, and the executable-to-relational proof bridge.

An empty target value does not answer whether the target cell exists. After a non-value computation, an absent target remains absent, while a present target is cleared in place and remains present-empty. Both states project to the same stored-value view and the same empty delta prior. Conversely, quiet no-value, target rejection, and inherited poison can produce the same exact empty state while retaining different delta, error, and dependency provenance. Exact application, stored value, and report delta are therefore three different observations; none can be reconstructed from either of the others.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/StringApplication.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/StringApplication.lean) introduces only the exact state of one already-addressed root String target: `absent`, `presentEmpty`, or `presentValue StoredString`. Accepted outcomes create or replace the value. No-value, target error, and poison clear an existing target in place and do not create an absent target. The older [`PriorStringTarget`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/StringComputation.lean) remains the correct smaller input for delta reporting and deliberately collapses absent with present-empty. [`Proofs/StringApplication.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/StringApplication.lean) states the complete transition independently as `StringTargetApplies`, proves soundness and completeness of `applyTo`, determinism, exact clearing and placement preservation for every outcome without an applied value, agreement with the older stored-value view, and both information-loss non-laws. [`Conformance/StringApplication.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/StringApplication.lean) locks all twelve outcome/prior classes plus accepted equality.

Evidence status: the compact root-String projection derives exact state through the live core transition rather than a second local rule. Its nine retained cases actively calibrate accepted writes over absent, stale-filled, and equal-filled targets and target errors over absent, stale-filled, and equal-filled targets. The archived qualified direct-cascade raw unit carries no-value over absent and filled producer targets, and the removed complete binder agreed with that unit before compact migration; ordinary compact replay intentionally projects those observations to value-only state. No retained project-local case starts with a present-empty target. The internal poison tag is also not directly observable in the retained public outputs; downstream clearing after producer failure is only an indirect witness.

Limit: the complete exact-state table is only partially externally calibrated. Accepted, no-value, target-error, and poison over an initially present-empty target remain `external evidence pending`, as do direct public poison application over absent or filled targets. This capsule assumes one already-resolved root target and does not model ancestor creation, general `Document` mutation, raw-text preservation, repeatable addressing, scheduling, multi-target atomicity, alternatives, or implicit validation.

## LF19 — precondition skipping precedes body poison-on-read

> Date: 2026-07-18. Sections: §11. Basis: [`spec/09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md), read-only kernel computation control flow, a12-dmkits IF77/IF85 and its tri-differential precondition/poison controls, red/green conformance, and trusted outcome/application/dependency proofs.

A computation whose common precondition is cleanly not true produces quiet no-value without evaluating its body. A formally invalid body operand therefore cannot poison that computation under that decision. With the same body and input but a holding decision, the operand is read and poison is preserved. Both outcomes can clear the same stale target and produce the same exact present-empty state, yet a later dependent computation observes quiet no-value as clean empty and observes poison as formal invalidity. Delta or target placement alone cannot recover that distinction.

Lean treatment: [`StringComputationStep.evaluateOutcomeWhen`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/StringCascade.lean) consumes the checked [`ComputationConditionResult`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ComputationCondition.lean). Clean `notTrue` returns `.noValue` before the body evaluator is called, `holds` delegates to the existing outcome evaluator, and `poison cause` preserves that cause without consulting the body. The arbitrary-step equations make the no-read property structural, while the consumed-invalid-field theorem connects a holding decision to the existing poison-on-read account. The separating non-law reuses exact application and the dependency overlay rather than introducing another state, scheduler, relation, or evidence layer.

Limit: the integrated condition AST now contains direct presence and ordered `And`/`Or`; LF21 owns the connective boundary, while LF22 owns ordered alternatives and remaining-alternative skipping. Comparisons, paths, and repeatable contexts remain future semantic clauses. No retained *local* packet contains a computation precondition, so project-local replay remains `external evidence pending`. The upstream confirmation is nonetheless specific and observes this claim's discriminator: a12-dmkits locks the precondition-cleared-propagates-as-EMPTY side in `adapter.laws.CascadeClearedDependencyDiffTest` (IF77) against the operand-invalidity boundary of IF85, with `adapter.laws.ComputationPreconditionDiffTest` on the precondition path directly. Composition triage 2026-07-25 therefore classifies the reachability claim `confirmed-probe` upstream and pending only for local retention; earlier wording cited IF77/IF85 without naming a test class, which is insufficient under the hypothesis rule in [`SOURCES.md`](SOURCES.md). Any future calibration is a separate approval-gated decision, not part of this semantic capsule.

## LF20 — computation presence reverses only clean truth

> Date: 2026-07-18. Sections: §3, §11. Basis: [`spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md`](../spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md), [`spec/03-empty-and-required.md`](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md), [`spec/09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md), read-only kernel presence-read behavior, a12-dmkits' direct presence/poison differential controls, red/green conformance, and trusted observation and computation-condition proofs.

Computation `FieldFilled` and `FieldNotFilled` are complements only for clean observations. Empty means not filled, a clean value means filled regardless of its scalar value, and both predicates preserve an ordinary formal invalidity as poison. Validation-scoped requiredness is the deliberate exception at the phase boundary: required-only empty remains empty in computation, whereas requiredness attached beside an ordinary finding does not hide that poison. Therefore neither raw presence nor Boolean negation determines computation presence.

Lean treatment: [`ComputationCondition.eval`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ComputationCondition.lean) reads one already-resolved checked field through `observeCell .computation` and returns `holds`, clean `notTrue`, or the exact poison cause. [`Proofs/ComputationCondition.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/ComputationCondition.lean) characterizes empty, clean value, required-only, ordinary-invalid, and exact-poison behavior and proves the nearest non-law: both presence predicates agree on poison rather than negating one another.
