[`Conformance/ComputationCondition.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/ComputationCondition.lean) also locks the second instance of the mechanism: a quietly cleared dependency satisfies downstream `FieldNotFilled`, while a poisoned dependency makes the same predicate poison.

Limit: this is a parser-independent, non-repeatable, already-resolved presence capsule. Ordered connectives and first-match alternatives now compose these leaves as described in LF21 and LF22, but the capsule does not lower DSL paths, evaluate comparisons, enforce model-level alternative legality, schedule computations, expose a public protocol, or claim that the external kernel reports the internal cause identity. Local portable differential evidence remains pending.

## LF21 — computation connectives are ordered control flow, not a commutative truth algebra

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §3, §11. Basis: [`spec/09-computations.md` §3.2](../spec/09-computations.md#32-an-invalidity-clear-poisons), read-only kernel composite-condition control flow, a12-dmkits IF85 and `ComputePoisonReadDiffTest`, red/green conformance, and trusted computation-condition proofs.

Computation `And` and `Or` determine which field reads happen, so they cannot reuse validation's commutative strong-Kleene result tables. Evaluation is left-to-right. `And` evaluates its right operand only after a holding left; a clean not-true left decides not-true. `Or` evaluates its right operand only after a clean not-true left; a holding left decides holds. A poison encountered on the left aborts either connective immediately, and a poison from the right is preserved only when that operand is reached.

The closest Boolean-looking rewrite is therefore unsound in the presence of poison. `notTrue And poison` is clean not-true, while `poison And notTrue` is poison. Likewise, `holds Or poison` holds, while `poison Or holds` poisons. Operand order is an observable part of the computation semantics and must be preserved by an interpreter, refactoring tool, or optimizer unless a proof establishes that the moved operand cannot poison.

Lean treatment: [`ComputationCondition`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ComputationCondition.lean) now has recursive `and` and `or` constructors whose evaluator matches the left result before recursing into the right. [`Proofs/ComputationCondition.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/ComputationCondition.lean) states all six branch equations and proves the two general operand-order non-laws. [`Conformance/ComputationCondition.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/ComputationCondition.lean) realizes both non-laws with filled, empty, and formally invalid direct-presence fields and feeds the ordered `And` decision through the existing precondition-before-body boundary.

Limit: the current leaf fragment contains direct presence only. It has no concrete clean comparison-unknown clause, quantifier scan, checked path lowering, general read trace, or public protocol. LF22 composes this result into ordered alternatives without widening the leaf language. The exact poison cause is an internal semantic distinction, not an externally reported kernel field. No retained compact bundle exercises computation preconditions or connective order, so this capsule remains `external evidence pending`; the existing a12-dmkits tri-differential is research provenance rather than project-local portable evidence.

## LF22 — computation selection is first-match, but implicit validation is all-alternatives

> Date: 2026-07-19; amended 2026-07-21, 2026-07-26, and 2026-07-30. Sections: §11. Basis: [`spec/01-data-model.md` §3.2](../spec/01-data-model.md#32-computations), [`spec/09-computations.md` §§5–6](../spec/09-computations.md#5-where-a-computation-runs--scope-and-the-parallel-join), kernel computation-table documentation plus [`CalculationUtils.expandCommonPrecondition`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-calc/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/calc/internal/util/CalculationUtils.java), [`CalculationDependencies`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-calc/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/calc/internal/util/CalculationDependencies.java), [`CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/calc/CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder.java), the [`ICalculationInfo`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-tool-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/tool/a12internal/api/ado/serviceparams/ICalculationInfo.java) selection contract, and terminal [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative` template](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st); accepted a12-dmkits `4ebe84fc5e9a342c042e4b0f488f416a6e7771e1`; red/green conformance; and trusted selection, same-target assembly, and structural proofs.

One computation scans its alternatives in declaration order. Clean not-true continues, a holding alternative selects its operation and makes the suffix irrelevant, poison aborts and makes the suffix unread, and exhausting the list yields clean no-match. **That order survives Groovy's split route, which was an unexamined gap until 2026-07-25:** above ten alternatives per computed field the generator chunks them, and the chunk calls are `||`-joined with each chunk returning false at its end, so short-circuiting preserves authored order across chunk boundaries. Locked upstream by `CalculationClosureSplitDiffTest` at a12-dmkits `dda6ba01` with the winner placed in the first chunk, a later chunk, and last position. A chunking defect there would silently change which alternative wins, so this route mattered more than its obscurity suggested — and no corpus reaches the threshold, alternative counts peaking at seven over 316 computations. Selection ends before the operation runs: if the selected operation itself later produces no value or poison, no later alternative is tried.

Several computations targeting one field reuse that mechanism rather than adding a first-nonempty combiner. [`CalculationDependencies`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-calc/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/calc/internal/util/CalculationDependencies.java) groups their information into an encounter-ordered list, [`CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/calc/CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder.java) distributes each common precondition and concatenates every row, and [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st) continues the target-instance loop unconditionally after a selected operation, poison, or runtime failure. A false guard can therefore fall through into a later computation, but a selected operation that stores nothing still ends the combined scan. Accepted a12-dmkits [`SPEC-2026-07-26-03`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-26-03--same-target-computations-flatten-into-one-first-selected-table) now locks the separating empty-String copy and all-empty concatenation on both kernel strategies plus JVM and Node; the former Number cases did not separate the accounts because their empty-first arms were unselected through false guards.

The same selector cannot implement the computation's generated validation rule. Runtime computation resolves overlapping holding preconditions to the first declaration, but generated validation retains one guarded mismatch disjunct per alternative. With overlapping guards, a stored value can equal the first operation while a later holding operation differs, so computation and validation deliberately take different paths. Agreement therefore needs an explicit mutual-exclusivity assumption or another sufficient condition; it is not true by construction.

Lean treatment: [`ComputationAlternative.expandCommonPrecondition`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ComputationCondition.lean) maps a present common condition to the left side of each guarded alternative and is identity when absent. It preserves operation payloads and declaration order. Its laws prove that a holding common guard preserves the original first-match result, a clean non-holding guard makes every table no-match without reaching alternative guards, and poison aborts at the first expanded guard. [`ComputationAlternative.selectFirst`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ComputationCondition.lean) remains operation-neutral and returns `noMatch`, `selected operation`, or exact-cause `poison` without evaluating the payload. Its original proof spine gives the empty and three head equations, characterizes no-match, and checks declaration-order non-laws for holding/holding and holding/poison pairs. [`StringAlternativeComputation`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/StringAlternatives.lean) composes expansion and selection once with the existing resolved String step while keeping one target, target policy, and prior state outside the alternatives. The checked String run now consolidates repeated targets at their first target position, appends every checked row in encounter order, proves exact resolved-row concatenation, and delegates the consolidated table to that same selector; result projection and application then distinguish a selected empty clear from an unselected fall-through without another computation-boundary decision. [`LiteralNumberComputation`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean) is the first checked common-condition consumer and models the complete nonempty literal-table shape as an optionally guarded singleton or a guarded two-or-more table. An unguarded singleton with no common condition selects directly; every present guard uses the shared selector, while generated validation omits a nonexistent singleton guard and lowers a common condition once between `FieldFilled(target)` and the complete mismatch body. Before either phase lowering, the checked guard traversal rejects the exact target ID and preserves common versus one-based alternative origin. Trusted laws cover every nested target reference, singleton selection, guard-table selection, same-target row order, and tolerance erasure; executable separators cover singleton own/common false guards, singleton and wider scale/tolerance admission, common/first/third guard positions, a reached third alternative, common outcome branches, same-target selected-empty/false-guard/value controls, exact source-relative clearing/application, and repeatable-reference rejection.

Limit: the general executable surface represents already-model-legal guarded String tables, accepts only already-resolved common conditions, and totalizes an empty list without claiming it is authorable. The checked common-condition consumer is deliberately limited to a nonrepeatable literal-Number table with direct presence/connective guards. Its sum type closes the nonempty cardinality and guard-presence split, but general lowering still must reject zero/default authoring at the wider source boundary and enforce target and common-condition legality beyond this fragment. Operation-side target rejection now exists for a separate checked plain numeric operation but is not yet integrated into the table or generated rule. Checked String-table guard integration for comparisons and quantifiers, repeatable scope, cross-family scheduling, expression-valued generated validation, and public protocol exposure remain open. The accepted String separator closes same-target external correspondence, while common-precondition behavior and target-reference admission remain source-grounded and `external evidence pending`. No evidence, support, protocol, harness, governance, dependency, or parallel table framework was added.

## LF23 — partial validation skips filtered rules before two relevance decisions

> Date: 2026-07-19; amended 2026-07-26 and 2026-08-09. Sections: §12. Basis: [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md` §5](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md#5-full-vs-partial-validation), kernel 30.8.1 public contracts, runtime routing, and Java/Groovy/JavaScript-VK rule templates at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; a12-dmkits partial-validation tests and interpreter through reviewed IF202 revision `6039fd3e`, plus the nested row-domain locks at `752054077be62be441b119af14efc97c6baf13d9`; red/green conformance; and trusted flat-relevance, filter-gate, checked preliminary-group, and outermost-row projection proofs.

Kernel 30.8.1 partial validation first skips every rule containing a `Having` filter, before iteration, correlation, or condition evaluation. **Not before error-field relevance, and the earlier wording here overreached** (Batch C triage, 2026-07-25): error-field selection is realised outside the rule method, where the partial-validation generator keys rules by error-field number and emits a switch on it, so that dispatch necessarily precedes the in-body skip. The real order is dispatch → skip → iteration → filter → condition. The mis-ordering is **knowingly unobservable** because both paths yield no finding, and the one gate that does precede the skip only logs, so nothing escapes. It changes nothing about the Lean treatment: the universal law states the load-bearing fact correctly — filtered implies skipped, independent of relevance — so it is this prose, not the formalisation, that was wrong. Full validation evaluates the same rule. An unfiltered rule then has two independent relevance decisions: an irrelevant explicit error field skips the rule, while an out-of-set referenced field contributes validation `unknown` after the rule is admitted. With clean filled fields `E` and `O`, error field `E`, condition `FieldFilled(E) Or FieldFilled(O)`, and only `O` relevant, read masking alone would yield `unknown Or true` and fire; the error-field gate skips the rule.

Lean treatment: [`FlatCondition.evalSelected`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/FlatValidation.lean) owns the recursive relevance-aware evaluator. [`FlatCondition.evalPartial`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/PartialValidation.lean) uses `FlatRuleFilterPresence.admits`; `.filtered` returns `FlatPartialResult.skipped` without forcing the deferred error-field query, while `.unfiltered` applies the existing error-field gate and leaf masking. The marker records the preceding elaboration fact without representing or executing `Having`. `FlatPartialResult.skipped | evaluated Verdict` preserves skipped, evaluated-not-fired, and evaluated-unknown. [`CheckedResolvedValidationRule.evalPartial`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/ValidationRule.lean) derives that marker from the complete checked tree, augments the caller's wildcardable scope with `FlatFieldDecl.isGlobal`, applies the explicit error-field gate, rejects an admitted addressed condition from the scalar route, and sends an admitted scalar verdict through the sole message emitter. [`CheckedDocument.applyPartialGeneratedPreliminaryForScope`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/CheckedIndexPreliminary.lean) owns global augmentation and normalization for every prepared addressed rule. Its exclusive addressed-input variant applies the resulting required and relevance-scoped duplicate-index annotations to ordinary partial field reads, while cause-free suppressed defaults remain a distinct unavailable outcome that evaluates as UNKNOWN rather than acquiring a fabricated formal cause; [`checkedPartialPreliminary_available_read_of_index_annotation`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/CheckedIndexPreliminary.lean) pins the available-read annotation bridge. [`CheckedDocument.validationRowEnvironments`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/CheckedDocument.lean) keeps the outermost level physical, then recursively adds child row 1 only beneath a parent with no concrete child. [`CheckedDocument.validationAddressedCell`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/CheckedStarDocument.lean) observes an exact projected implicit address as clean absent input without adding a row or widening physical reads. [`ValidationRelevanceScope.coversFieldAtBoundLevels`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/StarPath.lean) owns the rule-run gate separately from exact leaf relevance: one relevant error field or ancestor must project onto the error path, coordinates at levels bound by the checked reference-derived iteration plan must match, and deeper coordinates are ignored. [`evalOrdinaryRepeatablePartial`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/ValidationRule.lean) prepares its certificate once after the whole-rule filter skip and applies that gate per concrete or implicit validation environment; an admitted row still treats a nonrelevant reached source as UNKNOWN. A group leaf calls the preliminary view's sole selected-slice fold; `ValidationRelevanceScope.groupRelevance` derives none, partial, or full coverage from the normalized entity set and complete declared-descendant coverage, including wildcards across deeper repeatable levels, so the rule evaluator cannot relabel the complete document. The same rule owner exposes scalar, validation-row, and once plans; full once evaluation derives an all-one repeatable error address without reading or inserting the target row and retains the root admitted-value-content gate, while a partial once plan binds zero error-gate levels, admits any relevant instance on the error path, defaults the pointer to row 1, and bypasses root content after admission. The partial numeric leaf bridge admits ordinary atoms plus unfiltered checked Number aggregates and delegates the latter's concrete-versus-all-rows relevance to the existing aggregate owner. RNU retains canonical concrete topology order, appends only implicit validation descendants, selects the deepest key parent independently of authored component order, and prepares one branch-independent relation per default or authored-`@From` scope from rows whose complete composite key is relevant before any key read. Relative concrete/implicit result order is a deterministic Lean account; upstream sorted observations establish row membership and pointers but leave emission order unverified. Relevance remains call-local rather than a fabricated `FormalCause`; stars, group/index auto-checks, parallel identity, and computation still use physical topology. Concrete locks separate global from non-global scalar admission, repeatable row-exact gate from path-only over-admission, repeatable row skip from evaluated UNKNOWN, zero-bound once admission at error row 1 from error row 2 and no error-path selection, `And` from decisive `Or`, physical immutable row order, empty outer topology from a phantom, recursive implicit descendants from materialized rows, arbitrary missing environments from projected implicit ones, full root content from a contentless once frame, exact cell relevance from aggregate extent, global cross-parent RNU from per-parent `@From`, both mixed-depth key orders, equal from distinct ancestor keys under default and ancestor `@From` scope, complete from incomplete composite-key relevance, relevance-scoped duplicate index reads from partner omission, nonrelevant filled group content from a selected empty peer, and reached formal/silent group errors from clean content. The checked rule type carries no declared-group input from which runtime admission could derive a rival scope, and its owned iteration scope comes from the complete condition tree. Trusted laws retain the universal filtered skips, establish global-pattern augmentation, pin the admitted scalar rule to the existing relevance-aware condition tree and emitter, prove that a one-level validation scope equals physical enumeration, make iteration-bound error-path nonrelevance read-free, establish that a once plan binds zero error-gate levels, establish depth-independent RNU admission, characterize partial RNU result projection, derive group relevance from the prepared view, and prevent a repeatable no-scope rule from collapsing into scalar or validation-row execution.

Checked boundaries: a filtered rule may fire under full validation while partial validation skips it despite universal relevance. For an unfiltered rule, a relevant error field does not imply agreement with full evaluation when another reference is excluded; a direct comparison can be partial-unknown but full-fired, while a relevant true `Or` branch can decide despite a non-relevant sibling. A relevant unfiltered instance also bypasses the full-validation content gate.

Limit: the checked marker is discovered from already-elaborated sources but this capsule does not evaluate `Having`. Repeatable partial execution admits flat, direct repeatable-presence, ordinary scalar group presence, ordinary numeric, unfiltered checked Number-aggregate, and nested RNU leaves through its validation-row/once plans; every other addressed leaf family remains structurally unsupported. Partial multi-entity/starred group lists remain under [`SG13`](SEMANTICS-GAPS.md#sg13--group-list-and-group-count-completion), while generated checks, semantic-index/parallel joins, and whole-document collection retain their concrete-row owners. Global augmentation is represented for arbitrary wildcardable field paths, but executable cases do not yet establish every repeatable-global authoring combination. a12-dmkits IF202 and the maintained partial/global/row-content/RNU differentials are strong research provenance, but no project-local portable observation covers this family, so it remains `external evidence pending`. The canonical spec already owns the behavior; [`SOURCES.md`](SOURCES.md) records the routes without creating an outbound feedback-loop ledger entry.

## LF24 — direct Number ordering uses the same directional fixed-right comparison

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §2, §5, §12. Basis: [`spec/03-empty-and-required.md`](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md), [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md`](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md), [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md), kernel 30.8.1 comparison and field-policy mechanisms at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a12-dmkits empty/order/polarity/precision controls at revision `0f1bb3601b5765265df0e221c8c1989645d257e1`, red/green conformance, and trusted directional-polarity proofs.

Direct Number field-to-literal `<` and `>=` do not need a second evaluator. They consume the same exact rational operand and comparison-local fillability as equality/inequality, normalize both sides independently to scale 19 with `HALF_UP`, and differ only in the direction that could falsify a true condition. An empty Number substitutes zero; `<` is omission-typed for either signedness because filling can grow the left operand, while `>=` over that zero is omission-typed only for a signed field because only it can shrink. Filled zero is fixed and therefore VALUE when `>= 0` fires. Formal invalidity remains unknown, and the independent full-validation empty-row gate can still suppress the comparison before substitution.

Lean treatment: [`FlatComparison.number`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/FlatValidation.lean) reuses the six-case `NumericComparisonOp`, but checked flat lowering accepts exactly `equal`, `notEqual`, `less`, and `greaterEqual` for its reduced Number-field/literal surface. Boolean and Confirm remain limited to equality and inequality, direct String remains equality-only, and `lessEqual`/`greater` remain outside that checked flat surface even though the general checked numeric-expression consumer admits them. Numeric literals and ordering are statically scale-exempt, while declared field scale still belongs to formal input policy. [`emptyNumberLessFiring_is_omission`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericComparison.lean) and [`emptyNumberGreaterEqualFiring_polarity`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericComparison.lean) state the reusable empty-field consequences.

Limit: this finding's reduced checked-flat route closes all six comparisons for one nonrepeatable Number field against one fixed scale-erased literal; LF34 separately closes an internal checked two-expression arithmetic route that retains authored scale metadata. Tolerance, public JSON support, and a general order-duality rewrite remain separate. Committed a12-dmkits controls and kernel source inspection establish the research basis, but the local compact evidence bundle has no direct empty-Number ordering observation, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`. The canonical spec already stated the behavior exactly; it was explicitly audited and required no `spec/` change or [`A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md) queue entry.

## LF25 — authored rounding is a bounded two-stage operation

> Date: 2026-07-19; extended 2026-07-23. Sections: §5. Basis: corrected [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §§2 and 5](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#2-rounding), kernel 30.8.1 grammar, checking, scale propagation, runtime rounding, and operand-preservation mechanisms at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a12-dmkits `NumberLawsTest`, `RoundingLawsTest`, `RoundDiffTest`, and `SingleArgRoundDiffTest` at revision `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, reviewed arithmetic-wrapper and fillability differential revision `f9e591a54aa4c319baa42dfb76610d7571be5ecc`, direct-Base-Year correction revision `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`, red/green conformance, and trusted rounding-bound and wrapper-delegation proofs.

Both rounding syntaxes accept an optional decimal-places argument from zero through fourteen inclusive; omission means exactly zero. The `…Value(field[, n])` forms are not integer-only siblings and do not have different arithmetic. Every form first rescales its operand to scale 19 with decimal `HALF_UP`, then applies the requested target scale with `FLOOR` for `RoundDown`, `CEILING` for `RoundUp`, or `HALF_UP` for `RoundAccounting`. The result's static scale is the requested places count.

That first step changes the strongest valid direction law. Target-scale floor is at or below the scale-19 pre-rounded operand and target-scale ceiling is at or above it, but neither relation is universally true against a raw higher-precision rational. An exact value just below one can pre-round to one before zero-place floor and therefore produce a value greater than its raw input; the dual value just above one can pre-round to one before ceiling and produce less than its raw input. The Lean enum consequently names the mathematical modes `floor`, `ceiling`, and `halfUp` instead of perpetuating the ambiguous surface words “down” and “up.”

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/NumericRounding.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericRounding.lean) makes illegal places unrepresentable with `RoundingPlaces = Fin 15`, reuses one decimal `HALF_UP` primitive for comparison and rounding, and keeps the operation pure over exact `Rat`. The existing pure, checked-validation, and computation result domains each map only their available amount through that primitive and preserve their distinct unavailable, domain-failure, formal-cause, poison, and directional-fillability branches. [`Elaboration/NumericValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericValidation.lean) admits rounding at ordinary arithmetic operand positions over an already-checked complete numeric-operation child after immediate-literal rejection; the shared total evaluator maps that child's result once rather than defining another wrapper evaluator. Both expression and `…Value` surface spellings normalize to the same semantic node. [`Proofs/NumericRounding.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericRounding.lean) proves floor/ceiling bounds against the correct pre-rounded input plus generic result-domain preservation; [`Proofs/NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericExpression.lean) exposes the exact recursive body-check delegation; and [`Proofs/NumericValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericValidation.lean) provides one mode-parametric whole-body delegation law plus generic lowering and totality rather than restating each comparison table. The pure and checked conformance matrices separate both signs, all three modes, the `0`/`14`/`15` authoring boundary, omitted zero, fractional scale, arithmetic fillability, grouped field versus grouped literal, direct/grouped Base Year, body and enclosing division regions, power separation, nested wrapper order, expression-valued-extremum consumption, pre-rounding of a multi-term result, empty OMISSION, malformed UNKNOWN, right-side evaluation, result-scale admission, computation poison, target self-reference, generated validation, and both nearest false raw-input bounds.

Limit: the pure operation remains target-independent, and the checked extension closes a wrapper at the root or an ordinary arithmetic operand position over another admitted complete numeric operation, including expression-valued operand-list extrema. Concrete parsing, richer target construction, rendering, stored-form equality, public protocol support, and a second rounding meaning for computation metadata remain outside. The enclosing checker is not uniformly transparent: division contributions cross every reached function subtree, while each function node separates an outer direct-left power and preserves violations inside its own body. a12-dmkits's exact reviewed revisions supply dual-kernel provenance for root/enclosing forms and source-aligned generic nested representation, but this repository retains no portable rounding observation, so external correspondence remains pending. The peer prose, catalog, typed-read surface, and focused controls were reconciled under accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-07`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-07--rounding-accepts-zero-places-and-preserves-the-two-stage-mode) at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`; accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-23-05`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-23-05--numeric-operation-wrappers-compose-over-admitted-value-functions) records the already-satisfied nested value-function boundary without another upstream request.

## LF26 — every arithmetic node has a significant-digit boundary

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5. Basis: corrected [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §§1 and 5](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#5-internal-precision--the-constants-that-must-match-exactly), kernel `VkBigDecimal`, numeric parser checking, and `DivisionTransformer` at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits arithmetic implementation/prose/tests at revision `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green conformance, and trusted arithmetic laws.

The kernel passes the same decimal `MathContext(50)`, whose default mode is `HALF_UP`, to addition, subtraction, multiplication, division, and power. The former spec and a12-dmkits claim that `+` and `−` stay exact is therefore false. Precision means significant digits, not fractional scale, and the boundary occurs independently at every evaluated arithmetic node. Consequently, exact-rational ring laws cannot be transferred wholesale: addition and multiplication remain commutative, but addition is not associative. With `H = 10^50`, `(H + -H) + 0.6 = 0.6` while `H + (-H + 0.6) = 1`.

The evaluated arithmetic tree can also differ from the authored tree. The exact ordered one-pass rewrite and its current authored/lowered Lean account are recorded in [LF32](#lf32--division-lowering-is-one-ordered-post-order-pass-not-algebraic-normalization). Because each resulting node rounds, directly folding the authored AST is not a faithful default.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/NumericArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericArithmetic.lean) retains exact `Rat` as the value representation but computes the rational's exact decimal order of magnitude from numerator/denominator digit counts, derives a signed target scale, and applies decimal `HALF_UP` at precision 50 after every admitted `add`, `subtract`, `multiply`, or nonzero `divide` node. This handles large integer magnitudes, tiny fractional ties, and nonterminating quotients without floating point or a second decimal representation. Division checks the divisor before using `Rat./` and returns `NumericArithmeticResult.notEvaluated` on zero. [`Proofs/NumericArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericArithmetic.lean) proves zero preservation, addition/multiplication commutativity, subtraction-as-add-negation, multiplication by zero, division's exact definedness condition, and zero-dividend behavior. [`Conformance/NumericArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericArithmetic.lean) locks above-50 addition/subtraction, a 52-digit product, positive and negative tiny ties, a small exact control, zero-divisor non-evaluation, positive/negative repeating thirds, significant-digit rather than fractional-scale quotient precision, quotient consumption by a following multiplication, and both sides of the non-associativity witness.

The same source audit corrected neighboring authoring facts: checked expressions need known signed scale or unknown plus a multiplicative-constant capability; literals are not globally exempt; and a scale-0 base raised to a simple nonnegative constant is a narrow known-scale power case. a12-dmkits accepted both the runtime correction under [`SPEC-2026-07-19-08`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-08--every-arithmetic-node-uses-precision-50-half_up) and the checked-scale correction under [`SPEC-2026-07-19-09`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-09--numeric-scale-gating-tracks-signed-scale-and-constant-expandability) at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`.

Limit: this is a pure known-value seam, not a checked expression evaluator. LF34 now supplies the model-resolved validation projection and LF35 the declaration-resolved computation projection, while power uses the distinct staged algorithm recorded in LF27 rather than being collapsed into exact rational exponentiation. LF28 and LF29 subsequently add known-operand `+`/`−`/`×`/`÷` fillability plus domain-failure absorption, LF31 adds the independent static scale/capability summary, LF32 supplies the generic authored/lowered expression staging, and LF52 adds the resolved pure power fillability table. Concrete computation authoring, result-empty provenance, rendering, full target construction, and public protocol support remain open. Kernel source and existing multiplication/division differentials strongly ground the mechanism, and a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` closes the former extreme-addition/subtraction and per-node precision disagreement. This repository still retains no portable arithmetic observation, so correspondence for the new seam remains `external evidence pending`. No evidence or harness machinery was added.

## LF27 — power has two staged precision algorithms, not one algebraic shortcut

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5. Basis: corrected [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §§3 and 5](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#3-arithmetic-domain-failures-are-consumer-sensitive), kernel `VkBigDecimal.power` and reciprocal handling at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, the OpenJDK 21 `BigDecimal.pow(int, MathContext)` X3.274 contract, read-only a12-dmkits implementation/tests at revision `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green conformance, and trusted domain/staging laws.

Positive power is not exact rational exponentiation followed by one precision-50 round. Java scans the exponent's bits, rounds intermediate squares and accumulator multiplications with `HALF_UP` at working precision `50 + decimalDigits(exponent) + 1`, and only then rounds the result to precision 50. The difference is reachable with a legal 15-digit operand: `0.473471768303411 ^ 7` ends in `…3319`, while exact power followed by a single final round ends in `…3318`.

The kernel does not use Java's negative-exponent branch. It first calculates and precision-50-rounds `1 / base`, negates the exponent, and passes the rounded reciprocal through the positive algorithm. For `3 ^ -3`, that reciprocal-first route ends in `…7036`; raising first and taking the final reciprocal ends in `…7037`. The originally audited a12-dmkits JVM evaluator used the latter order even though its fillability walk used reciprocal-first reasoning and its exact `4 ^ -1` differential could not expose the defect; accepted revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` now implements and locks the reciprocal-first route and staged precision matrix.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/NumericArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericArithmetic.lean) factors the existing fixed precision through a total significant-digit helper, implements the OpenJDK 21 positive bit algorithm directly over `Rat`, admits only integral runtime exponents in inclusive `-1000..1000`, and routes negative exponents through the existing partial division before positive power. [`Proofs/NumericArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericArithmetic.lean) states rejection, zero exponent, zero-base negative quietness, and the reciprocal-first branch equation. [`Conformance/NumericArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericArithmetic.lean) locks staged power versus final-round-only power, the exact working-precision guard digit, reciprocal-first versus reciprocal-after-power, `0^0`, `0^-1`, `4^-1`, negative-base parity, defensive fractional quietness for an unchecked caller, and all four inclusive/exclusive range edges. Kernel authoring rejects fractional- or unknown-scale exponents. LF34 now connects the checked validation consumer to this evaluator and its static summary; LF35 connects the declaration-resolved computation consumer after preceding authoring admission.

Limit: this pins the current X3.274 numeric-value account, which Java documents as an approximation whose implementation may evolve; it is deliberately not a theorem equating power with exact rational exponentiation. LF52 subsequently adds the resolved pure directional table while keeping runtime invalidity in the same arithmetic outcome, LF34 adds checked validation, and LF35 adds declaration-resolved computation. Result-empty provenance, concrete computation authoring, rendering, and full target/scheduler integration remain separate. No portable project-local power observation exists, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`; the peer correction and differential matrix were accepted under [`SPEC-2026-07-19-10`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-10--power-preserves-staged-java-precision-and-reciprocal-first-negative-order) at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`. No evidence or harness machinery was added.

## LF28 — multiplication fillability needs joint movement, not only current signs

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5, §12. Basis: corrected [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md` §4](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md#4-the-directional-fill-machinery-behind-the-typing), kernel `VkBigDecimal` arithmetic-fillability methods at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits `ArithFill`, `ArithmeticFillTest`, and `ArithmeticFillPolarityDiffTest` at revision `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green conformance, and trusted fillability laws.

Arithmetic polarity metadata is a conservative two-direction abstract interpretation over valid current numeric values. Addition ORs corresponding directions; subtraction swaps the subtrahend's directions. Multiplication additionally combines each operand's directions with the other current sign, but that is not enough: grow/grow and shrink/shrink jointly make the product able to grow, while grow/shrink and shrink/grow jointly make it able to shrink. Thus two grow-only operands currently equal to zero produce a grow-only product even though both sign-scaled contributions are false. A sign-only propagation rule would silently type a downstream firing as VALUE instead of OMISSION.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericFillability.lean) owns `NumericFillability`, derives `NumericSign` from the same exact `Rat` operands consumed by arithmetic, and implements the exact `+`, `−`, and `×` formulas independently of numeric rounding. [`Proofs/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericFillability.lean) proves all-fixed preservation, addition and multiplication commutativity, subtraction as addition with the right directions swapped, its fixed-operand consequences, and preservation/swap/fixedness under multiplication by fixed positive, negative, and zero factors. [`Conformance/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericFillability.lean) separately locks the same-direction and mixed-direction zero-sign joint terms and carries positive/negative/zero fixed-factor results into the existing fixed-right comparison polarity evaluator.

Limit: this first capsule accepts valid known operands and deliberately has no expression result that could absorb `NumericArithmeticResult.notEvaluated`. LF29 subsequently adds that smallest combined result and division's reciprocal-direction transformation; LF52 extends the same result with the separate conservative power table. Empty provenance remains the responsibility of the operand-producing clause rather than a third fillability bit. Correspondence remains `external evidence pending`; the formulas, peer controls, and power probes were reconciled under accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-11`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-11--arithmetic-fillability-uses-joint-terms-and-conservative-power-dispatch) at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`. No AST, protocol, target, evidence, or harness machinery was added.

## LF29 — division uses a reciprocal only for fillability and absorbs domain failures

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5, §11, §12. Basis: [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §§3 and 5](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#3-arithmetic-domain-failures-are-consumer-sensitive), [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md` §4](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md#4-the-directional-fill-machinery-behind-the-typing), corrected [`spec/09-computations.md` §§3.1–3.2](../spec/09-computations.md#31-a-precondition-clear-cascades-as-empty), kernel `VkBigDecimal.divide`, reciprocal-fillability, rounding, calculation-cache, generated calculation-alternative, and result-projection paths at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits `ArithFill`, `ExprEval`, `PolarityWalk`, `ComputationEvaluationLawsTest`, and `RuntimeLaws` at revision `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green conformance, and trusted absorption/sign laws.

Division has two deliberately different routes. The numeric value is the direct precision-50 quotient `round50(a / b)`. Only directional metadata rewrites the divisor as a reciprocal and then reuses the multiplication table: reciprocal grow is `Sᵦ ∨ (b < 0 ∧ Gᵦ)`, reciprocal shrink is `Gᵦ ∨ (b > 0 ∧ Sᵦ)`, and its sign is the nonzero divisor's sign. Constructing a rounded reciprocal amount and multiplying would be wrong: direct `2 / 3` rounds its last retained digit up, while `2 × round50(1 / 3)` ends one unit lower at that precision. A current zero divisor is rejected before either metadata transformation or rational division, even when its fillability says it could later move away from zero.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericFillability.lean) adds `NumericArithmeticOutcome`, whose success constructor keeps the rounded `Rat` and `NumericFillability` inseparable and whose `notEvaluated` constructor absorbs either child before a total arithmetic node or division runs. Division delegates its value to `divideNumeric` and sends only the divisor's transformed flags through `NumericFillability.multiply`. [`Proofs/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericFillability.lean) proves left/right absorption, exact zero-divisor definedness for known values, fixed/fixed preservation, preservation under a fixed positive divisor, direction swap under a fixed negative divisor, and fixedness of a zero fixed numerator under any nonzero divisor. [`Conformance/NumericFillability.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericFillability.lean) separates all four reciprocal sign/direction branches, zero-numerator joint terms, direct quotient order, zero-gate precedence, enclosing-operation absorption, and the resulting positive/negative-divisor comparison polarities.

The source audit also exposed a computation boundary outside this pure capsule. The originally pinned a12-dmkits public-API `RoundAccounting(div, 2)` probe established no fresh computed-field outcome and stale CLEARED, but its mapper omitted `formalErrorsInOperands` and never read the target from a dependent computation. Kernel source closes the semantic mechanism: numeric wrappers preserve the invalid number, the handler marks a Number target invalid and adds `berechnungsWertFehler`, and the calculation cache throws when a dependent reads it, causing that dependent target to become invalid in turn. A Date shift instead projects the same missing numeric amount to a valueless Date without adding that numeric error to the Date target. a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` accepted and locked these consumer-specific routes under [`SPEC-2026-07-19-12`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-12--division-by-zero-computations-poison-despite-a-cleared-delta). `NumericArithmeticOutcome.notEvaluated` records only the pure domain failure; it deliberately does not choose a validation, Number-target, or Date-consumer projection.

Limit: this remains a known-value arithmetic outcome, not checked syntax or a general effect type. LF32 supplies the generic authored/lowered tree, LF52 extends this outcome with resolved power fillability, LF34 supplies the model-resolved validation consumer with two-sided comparison and static-summary admission, and LF35 supplies the declaration-resolved computation projection. Concrete computation authoring, general target/store construction, result-empty provenance, rendering, and public protocol support remain separate. Project-local portable arithmetic-fillability evidence remains pending, and no evidence or harness machinery was added.

## LF30 — tolerance normalizes operands independently and closes gaps directionally

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5, §11, §12. Basis: pinned kernel 30.8.1 runtime/parser/computation-generation source at `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a read-only audit of a12-dmkits at `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green executable separators, and trusted tolerance laws.

The four fixed tolerance comparisons first normalize each operand independently to scale 19 with `HALF_UP`, then fire only when the absolute normalized gap is strictly greater than `1`, `2`, `5`, or `10`. Rounding the already-computed difference is a different algorithm: with `ε = 49 / 10²¹`, operands `1 + ε` and `−ε` independently normalize to `1` and `0`, so range 1 stays quiet, while rounding their raw difference crosses the boundary.

Polarity reuses the directional inequality mechanism rather than equality's any-movement rule. If normalized left is smaller, only left growth or right shrinkage can repair an exceeded band; if left is larger, only left shrinkage or right growth can. Consequently, the same empty unsigned `0` under range 1 fires VALUE against `−2` but OMISSION against `+2`; the signed negative control is OMISSION. Truth is symmetric under swapping operands, and the direction flags swap with the operand roles.

Lean treatment: [`NumericComparison.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericComparison.lean) owns one shared `numericDifferenceFillCanClose` helper used by ordinary inequality and tolerance. [`NumericTolerance.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericTolerance.lean) owns the closed threshold type, independent normalization, strict truth predicate, two-sided fillability, unknown-preserving verdict, and the closed ordinary-or-tolerance dispatch consumed by checked validation. [`NumericValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericValidation.lean) reuses one model/path/authoring/lowering/read/failure/row-gate pipeline for both operations; only static scale admission and final primitive verdict dispatch vary. [`Proofs/NumericTolerance.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericTolerance.lean) proves positive thresholds, normalized-gap/truth symmetry, symmetry of the complete verdict under operand/fillability swap, unknown preservation, the exact closed boundary, fixed VALUE firing, and the grow-only closing-versus-widening polarity split. [`Proofs/NumericValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericValidation.lean) proves tolerance's universal scale-gate bypass and direct atom/literal delegation. [`Conformance/NumericTolerance.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericTolerance.lean) retains the pure four-band and normalization/polarity matrix; [`Conformance/NumericValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericValidation.lean) adds checked scale-bypass, one-pass-lowering, row-gate, formal/domain, strict-boundary, directional-polarity, and fail-closed separators without duplicating it.

The original audit found two root defects in a12-dmkits: its evaluator normalized after subtraction, and its polarity walk used `anyMove`; the then-existing signed-field tests could not separate either error. Revision `d6a08f5b53022d9caae35e6d842a2b7b22cd5b25` separately closed the loss of a computation alternative's declared tolerance operator during implicit-validation synthesis. Accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-13`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-13--tolerance-normalizes-operands-first-and-uses-directional-inequality-polarity) records the completed shared normalization/polarity correction and focused cross-host/kernel-route matrix at revision `7b39728eafdb53b0a69fcafd34817980bb900632`.

Limit: the checked local capsule is parser-independent and admits only the existing same-group, nonrepeatable plain Number-expression subset. It does not lower the four concrete tokens, admit numeric `BaseYear` or other number-like operations, synthesize implicit computation validation, support partial/repeatable evaluation, or expose a public protocol operation. No retained project-local kernel observation exercises tolerance, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`; no new harness was introduced.

## LF31 — numeric scale is signed static metadata, not runtime decimal representation

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5. Basis: [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §1](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#1-scale-gates----checked-at-parse-time), pinned kernel parser/type-summary source at `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a read-only a12-dmkits authoring/test audit at `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green conformance, and trusted static-summary laws.

A checked numeric expression carries two independent authoring facts: an exact signed decimal scale or unknown, and whether trailing zeros may expand that scale. The sign matters because an integer literal is stripped before its scale is recorded: `100` has scale `−2`, while authored `100.00` has scale `2`. Every numeric literal is expandable; fields use their declared nonnegative scale and are not. Addition and subtraction take the maximum known scale and AND the capabilities, multiplication adds known scales and ORs the capabilities, division becomes unknown and non-expandable, and explicit rounding fixes the requested scale and clears the capability. Power rejects an unknown or positive exponent scale; its result is known scale zero only for a scale-zero base and a syntactically simple nonnegative constant exponent, and otherwise remains unknown.

The unsuppressed `==`/`!=` gate accepts two known equal scales or a mismatch whose strictly smaller-scale side is expandable. This is value-preserving padding, never scale reduction. Hence a scale-2 field may compare with literal `0`, but a scale-0 field may not compare with authored `0.00`, in either operand order. Expandability also does not imply a known scale: multiplying a division result by a literal yields unknown scale with capability true, and exact comparison still rejects it. Ordering and tolerance bypass this gate; suppression, concrete operator dispatch, and diagnostics remain later checked-surface concerns.

Lean treatment: [`ScaleInfo`](../A12Kernel/Core.lean) was generalized in place from `exact Nat | unknown` to `exact Int | unknown`; no parallel signed-scale type was added. [`NumericScaleSummary`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericScale.lean) is a pure static abstraction separate from `Rat`, `NumericArithmeticOutcome`, fillability, and stored rendering. Its closed binary enum summarizes `+`, `−`, `×`, and `÷`; `power?` checks the exponent-scale precondition before deriving the narrow result; and `exactNumericScaleComparisonAllowed` owns only the unsuppressed exact gate. [`Proofs/NumericScale.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericScale.lean) proves binary-summary and gate symmetry, exact smaller-side characterization, field-only specialization, same-scale acceptance, and unknown rejection. [`Conformance/NumericScale.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericScale.lean) locks signed literals, both operand orders, the false global-literal-exemption rule, capability propagation, unknown-with-capability independence, rounding reset, and the narrow power cases.

The original a12-dmkits audit found that its scale-2-field tests against `0`, `0.0`, and `0.00` were all valid but non-separating, while prose generalized them incorrectly to global literal exemption. Its field-to-field precheck was already a correct specialization, and its AST retained enough field, literal, rounding, and arithmetic structure for one shared static summarizer. Accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-09`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-09--numeric-scale-gating-tracks-signed-scale-and-constant-expandability) records the corrected peer implementation, prose, and controls at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`; no duplicate queue entry is warranted.

Limit: the current [`SurfaceLiteral.number`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/Flat.lean) stores only `Rat` and has irreversibly erased authored scale, so this capsule deliberately does not bolt literal exceptions onto the flat elaborator. LF32's generic authored tree now retains decoded scale, braces/simple-constant classification, and operator form and consumes this algebra, while LF33 checks the plain division/power regions; model resolution, operation-valued wrappers, exact diagnostics, and a checked validation/computation consumer remain later work. Field-only correlation remains unchanged and is covered by the proved specialization. Static authoring acceptance is not runtime power-domain acceptance: a simple `1001` exponent can derive scale zero and still fail the separate runtime `±1000` check. The new clauses are source-grounded but lack the project-local portable separator matrix, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`; no protocol, evidence, target, parser, or harness machinery was added.

## LF32 — division lowering is one ordered post-order pass, not algebraic normalization

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5. Basis: corrected [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §§4–5](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#5-internal-precision--the-constants-that-must-match-exactly), pinned kernel grammar/checker, parse-tree traversal, `DivisionTransformer`, composite code generation, and precision-50 runtime source at `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a read-only a12-dmkits audit at `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green structural/value separators, and trusted local lowering equations.

After authoring checks and before code generation, the kernel walks the original operation tree post-order exactly once. At each multiplication it examines only the immediate, already-lowered children whose root is division. Ordinary factors retain their order and precede the extracted numerators; numerators and denominators retain division encounter order; multi-factor products are left-folded. The new products are not visited again. Braces reset the earlier division-count and power-nesting regions, but they do not hide a divided factor from this later pass. Therefore `{b / c} * a` becomes `{a * b} / c`, a nested extracted numerator may leave a direct division inside a newly created product, and a second pass would be a different evaluator.

This staging is numerically observable because each node rounds independently at precision 50. A counterfactual direct fold of `3 * {1 / 3}` yields `1 − 10⁻⁵⁰`; the required lowered tree `{3 * 1} / 3` yields exactly `1`. Multiplying the result by `10³¹` leaves a scale-19-visible gap of `10⁻¹⁹`, so the distinction can change a validation comparison after its own normalization. This also corrected the former pre-round example: `[q] / 3 * 3` becomes exact through division lowering and therefore cannot isolate target rounding's scale-19 pre-round; three independently divided thirds separated by addition do.

Lean treatment: [`NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericExpression.lean) keeps generic `AuthoredNumericExpr` and `LoweredNumericExpr` types separate. The authored tree stores `DecodedNumericLiteral { value, authoredScale }`, explicit groups, arithmetic, power, and bounded rounding. `summary?` consumes that tree before lowering, so a multiplication can remain unknown-but-expandable even when the lowered root is a non-expandable division. `lowerForEvaluation` performs the exact bottom-up local step; `LoweredNumericExpr.evalValue` reuses the existing partial precision-50 arithmetic, staged power, and two-stage rounding definitions. [`Proofs/NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericExpression.lean) records group transparency for summaries/lowering, group opacity for simple-power classification, all immediate divided-factor equations, the no-second-pass nested case, and the evaluator bridge. [`Conformance/NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericExpression.lean) locks authored-scale preservation, direct versus grouped power classification, factor order, two divided factors, addition/rounding/power boundaries, unknown capability, zero-domain failure, and the rounded non-law.

At the original audit revision, the a12-dmkits authoring and interpreter trees erased explicit grouping, carried no common static summary, evaluated the parsed arithmetic tree directly, and could not faithfully render a right division under multiplication. Accepted revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` closes those peer gaps through [`SPEC-2026-07-19-08`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-08--every-arithmetic-node-uses-precision-50-half_up), [`SPEC-2026-07-19-09`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-09--numeric-scale-gating-tracks-signed-scale-and-constant-expandability), and [`SPEC-2026-07-19-14`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-14--numeric-authoring-regions-are-structural-and-function-wrappers-remain-unclosed): per-node arithmetic, one-pass lowering, signed scale, constant expandability, and structured grouping/rendering now have maintained peer controls.

Limit: this is a parser-independent known-value expression seam, generic over atoms supplied by a caller. LF33 checks the plain division/power authoring restrictions, LF34 supplies model resolution plus checked validation, and LF35 supplies declaration-resolved computation. Operation-valued wrapper legality, exact source diagnostics/suppression, concrete computation authoring, Number rendering/store, and public exposure remain separate. The exact mechanism and separating values are source-grounded but have no retained portable kernel observation, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`; no protocol, evidence, target, parser, or harness machinery was added.

## LF33 — numeric authoring regions are compositional only over plain arithmetic

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §5. Basis: corrected [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#4-other-numeric-constraints), pinned kernel grammar and `CheckOperationImpl` at `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a read-only a12-dmkits audit at `2ceee778cbcbd16a63e456fb662d3b61a13c99a8`, red/green authoring separators, and trusted structural laws.

For parser-reachable plain arithmetic, division legality has one compositional account. Atoms expose zero divisions. Multiplication combines both open contributions; division combines them and adds one. Addition, subtraction, power, and grouping validate their children as fresh regions but expose zero upward. Any multiplication/division region above one is illegal, so `(a / b) / c` fails, `(a / b) + (c / d)` succeeds, and `{a / b} / c` succeeds without making the grouped quotient opaque to LF32's later evaluation rewrite.

Power legality is a separate syntactic rule: reject exactly a power whose direct left operand is another ungrouped power. The concrete grammar is left-associative, so this catches the reachable `a ^ b ^ c`; grouping the left power or exponent is legal, and separate powers may occur on both sides of multiplication. A generic AST can encode a direct-right ungrouped power that the parser cannot produce; accepting that shape is a checked reminder that grammar reachability remains a distinct elaboration precondition, not evidence for a symmetric kernel rule.

The kernel's operation-valued function behavior is not the same clean recursion. Its legacy listener descends through wrappers such as rounding, but a nested group/add/sub/power boundary aborts the enclosing walk through a control-flow exception. Source therefore predicts order-sensitive cases in which an early nested reset hides later factors; no focused portable probe currently closes that artifact. Treating every function as transparent or as a reset would both overclaim.

Lean treatment: [`NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericExpression.lean) adds one private structural scan and a closed `NumericAuthoringCheck` with accepted, each independent violation, their combination, and `outsideFragment`. The scan covers atoms, literals, groups, binary arithmetic, and power; any `.round` anywhere returns `outsideFragment`, which is no kernel-legality judgment. [`Proofs/NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericExpression.lean) proves single-division acceptance, additive and grouped separation, preservation of an illegal inner region, exact direct-left power rejection, grouped acceptance, the direct-right parser-shape boundary, and wrapper exclusion. [`Conformance/NumericExpression.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericExpression.lean) locks the wider separating matrix and simultaneous violations.

The original a12-dmkits typed tree did not retain grouping, and its MVK ledger delegated the two checks while summarizing division only as a calculation subtree. Accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-14`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-14--numeric-authoring-regions-are-structural-and-function-wrappers-remain-unclosed) records the corrected structural authoring regions and grouping boundary at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`. Operation-valued function wrappers remain deliberately unclosed in both accounts rather than being inferred from the plain-arithmetic recursion.

Limit: this capsule does not parse concrete syntax, prove grammar-shape reachability, reproduce source spans/localized diagnostics or their global precedence, model operation-valued functions, resolve fields, check scale/type/domain, or alter runtime evaluation. Its plain structural clauses are source-grounded but have no retained project-local observation, and the wrapper artifact is deliberately unclosed; no evidence or harness machinery was added.

## LF34 — checked numeric validation is an explicit staged consumer

> Date: 2026-07-19; extended 2026-07-21. Sections: §2, §3, §5, §10, §12. Basis: [`spec/03-empty-and-required.md` A.1/A.4](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md#part-a--2-non-specified-empty-values), [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §§1, 3–5](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md), [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md` §4](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md#4-the-directional-fill-machinery-behind-the-typing), pinned kernel preamble/comparison checker, transformer, and runtime source at `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits arithmetic/polarity controls through accepted revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`, red/green integrated conformance, and trusted projection/core-agreement laws.

The checked numeric-expression consumer remains deliberately one validation leaf, not a general expression framework. It accepts two expression operands and a closed dispatch over all six ordinary operators or the four fixed tolerance ranges, with at least one model-resolved Number field across the pair. Plain arithmetic includes `+`, `−`, `×`, `÷`, and power; every source must be nonrepeatable and in the evaluated row group, while cross-group, repeatable, non-Number, constant-only, and operation-valued shapes fail closed. Elaboration validates the model and both path sets, retains authored literal scale and grouping, applies the division-region/direct-left-power authoring rule and static summary independently before rewriting, rejects fractional/unknown-scale exponents, gates ordinary `==`/`!=` by the exact-scale rule unless the structured input explicitly suppresses `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, leaves ordering and tolerance scale-exempt, and then performs exactly one division-lowering pass per operand. The suppression matches the kernel's branch placement: it admits differing and unknown derived scales but bypasses no model, scope, type, expression-shape, or authoring check.

Runtime evaluation reuses the existing validation-phase Number read on both sides. An empty unsigned or signed Number becomes zero with its directional fillability, a clean Number is fixed, and a formal-invalid cell remains an exact-cause error. `Except FormalCause NumericArithmeticOutcome` keeps formal invalidity separate from pure arithmetic `notEvaluated` without introducing another effect hierarchy. The validation projection maps a formal error on either side to `Verdict.unknown`, a pure domain failure to `Verdict.notFired`, and two values to the scale-19 comparison with operand-local movement. The full-validation content gate runs before either expression, so an unreferenced sibling can still change the explicit `hasContent` premise without changing any expression atom.

The key two-sided discriminator compares filled zero on the left with empty unsigned zero on the right under `>=`: the values satisfy the condition, but the right operand can grow and clear it, so the firing is OMISSION; replacing the right operand with filled zero makes the same truth VALUE. Complementary `<` and `!=` cases separate a right operand that can grow away from the boundary from one that can shrink toward it. Power adds a more demanding composition separator: `0 ^ emptyUnsigned` and `0 ^ emptySigned` both evaluate to one, but the first is shrink-only and fires `>= 1` with OMISSION while the second is grow-only and fires with VALUE. Valid power delegates amount and directions together; `0 ^ -1` and exponent `1001` project to quiet domain failure; fractional exponents fail static admission; and direct-left nested power requires grouping. Both operand positions have independent one-pass-lowering, malformed, domain, authoring-region, kind, and scale-gate locks; equality-boundary cases distinguish strict `>` from inclusive `<=`, while pinned source establishes them as direct operator branches. The suppression matrix admits unequal field/literal scales, unequal field/field scales, and an unknown division result, then confirms that a nested-division authoring violation still fails. In a content-bearing, row-selected instance, empty `[U] == [U]` is intentionally OMISSION because the runtime reconstructs fillability for each read and the comparator performs no dependency alias analysis; an entirely empty full-validation row remains `notFired`. This alias outcome is source-inferred and remains external evidence pending. Trusted theorems prove direct atom/literal agreement with the shared low-level Number-field evaluator, reached power delegation, universal suppression admission, unchanged unsuppressed admission, and runtime irrelevance of the suppression flag, not equality of the two checked surface languages.

One richer internal choice is deliberately conservative rather than a kernel-correspondence claim: if one operand is formally invalid and the other has an arithmetic domain failure, the Lean projection retains `unknown`. The canonical rule says formal validity is prior, but the kernel runtime collapses both hidden failures to authored-message silence and does not establish the same internal precedence. The conformance locks both operand orders as a project refinement while the mixed kernel state remains open. The two-expression shape, six direct operators, scale rules, and directional polarity were already canonical, so no `spec/` change or new [`A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md`](A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md) entry was warranted.

Limit: this checked capsule does not admit concrete ordinary/tolerance syntax, `BaseYear`, operation-valued wrappers, partial validation, repeats, cross-group/global evaluation, kernel diagnostic codes, computations or their generated validation, target rendering/application, the public reference protocol, or portable local evidence. Its structured surface assumes a grammar-valid decoder and coherent literal value/authored-scale pairs; it does not prove those preceding parser obligations or reject every generic-AST shape that the concrete grammar cannot produce. Correspondence excludes mixed formal/domain precedence and otherwise remains `external evidence pending`; no evidence, protocol, harness, or governance code was added. The closed operator dispatch is the second demonstrated consumer of the exact checked result domain and replaces a parallel tolerance elaborator; resolution, static checks, lowering, reads, failure projection, and row gating remain single-source.

## LF35 — numeric computation preserves domain failure and read poison separately

> Date: 2026-07-19; extended 2026-07-21. Sections: §2, §3, §4, §5, §11. Basis: [`spec/03-empty-and-required.md`](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md), [`spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §3](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#3-arithmetic-domain-failures-are-consumer-sensitive), [`spec/09-computations.md` §§1–3](../spec/09-computations.md), pinned kernel `VkBigDecimal`, `CompositeOperation`, rounding, power, calculation-result, and calculation-cache source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits computation prose and implementation through `62d9fbd91cb14e73a9fa37375ca1c77cc67a9947`, red/green conformance, and trusted expression-outcome laws.

Number computation needs a result domain different from both validation and String computation. An empty Number operand—including required-only empty—contributes the real value zero, so the expression has no clean `noValue` arm and no full-validation content gate. Division by zero and runtime-invalid integral power instead produce the same arithmetic domain failure, which clean enclosing ordinary arithmetic and explicit rounding preserve until the target consumer classifies it; a later evaluated invalid read can still replace it with poison. A formally invalid field actually read is neither zero nor domain failure: it preserves its exact cause as computation poison. Clean false/no-match belongs to the already separate precondition and alternative-selection boundary and never enters this expression result.

Evaluation order matters even before a scheduler exists. Generated arithmetic is a Java receiver chain: the left numeric result is produced first, then the right argument is evaluated before the enclosing method call. A zero division is represented by the kernel's invalid numeric value rather than an exception, so a later right-side invalid-cell read can still poison; a left poison read throws and prevents the right subtree from replacing its cause. The Lean evaluator exposes these as ordered equations. Composition triage 2026-07-25 settled the *order* claim at its correct locus: the codegen backing bean `CompositeOperation.getFeldOperationCodeJava` emits a left-associated receiver chain — operand zero becomes the receiver and each later operand becomes a single-argument call whose method name derives from the `CalcOp` — and the method consults no target language, so the shape is single-sourced across the groovy, java, and java_script_vk dialects, which all interpolate that one property. Combined with guaranteed JLS receiver-before-argument order this fixes which of two poisoned reads is reached first, so no probe is required for the order account. Portable mixed domain/poison confirmation of the *result classes* remains pending.

Lean treatment: [`NumericComputationResult.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericComputationResult.lean) defines `NumericComputationResult = value | domainFailure | poison`, while [`NumericComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericComputation.lean) now has both the low-level declaration-resolved evaluator and a checked entry point for one nonrepeatable plain numeric operation. The checked route validates the model, resolves the Number target by ID and operand paths through the generic authored-tree traversal shared with numeric validation, rejects repeatable/wrong-kind operands and the target at every nesting depth, and applies the existing division-region/direct-left-power authoring check and static summary. Exact result-scale compatibility remains mandatory by default; the one explicit warning flag bypasses only that predicate and is retained in the certified core. Constant-only operations remain legal. The resulting well-formedness certificate universally excludes a direct target reference and proves the ordinary-gate-or-suppression disjunction before evaluation. Each atom retains its `FlatFieldDecl`; a complete structural preflight still defensively rejects every non-Number declaration before any data read, preventing empty wrong-kind substitution from being hidden by an earlier poison. The runtime order is the lowered-tree order: the checked `(Source / 2) * Later` separator becomes `(Later * Source) / 2`, so distinct poison causes establish that `Later` is reached first. Reached power values delegate to the shared staged `powerNumeric` evaluator; its `notEvaluated` arm becomes the existing computation `domainFailure`, so `0 ^ -1` and `2 ^ 1001` reach target invalidity without a power-specific result type. After evaluation, the certified suppression bit alone selects ordinary or warning-suppressed target classification; the target policy must carry proof-equal resolved scale and signedness, while its minimum fractional scale remains explicit because the flat declaration does not retain that fact. Ordinary binary nodes continue to share [`NumericScaleBinaryOp.evalValues`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericExpression.lean). The consumers do not share validation fillability/failure projection or a generic String/Number computation result because those meanings differ.

[`Proofs/NumericComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericComputation.lean) proves that every checked operation excludes its target, plus declaration-certified empty and required-empty zero, exact poison preservation, universal direct division-by-zero domain failure, exact reached-value power delegation, zero-to-negative computation failure, rounding and clean enclosing-arithmetic preservation on either side, admitted left-poison short-circuiting, the source-grounded right-poison-after-left-domain law, result separation, and context-independent structural rejection. [`Conformance/NumericComputation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericComputation.lean) locks checked nested self-reference, valid field-bearing and constant-only evaluation, wrong-kind/repeatable rejection, plain authoring failure, result-scale mismatch, clean values, all three result classes, requiredness, one-pass numeric lowering, valid and runtime-invalid power, rounding, both domain-failure operand positions, authored-versus-lowered poison order, and complete wrong-kind preflight. [`Conformance/NumericDependency.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericDependency.lean) locks the invalid-power expression-to-target-to-dependent-poison route.

Limit: this is checked parser-independent numeric authoring for one nonrepeatable operation, not a full computation table or concrete syntax. Later capsules closed operation-form rounding/`Abs` at ordinary arithmetic positions, nested wrappers, expression-valued operand-list extrema with per-call source boundaries, target-policy attachment, and expression-valued generated validation through the same checked route; richer declaration-driven target construction, repeats, scheduling, public protocol, and project-local portable evidence remain open. LF36 supplies ordinary and explicit warning-suppressed target classification, stored rendering, and prior-target delta; LF37 supplies exact one-address final application; LF38 supplies the cause-free outcome-to-dependency observation. [`spec/09-computations.md` §3.2](../spec/09-computations.md#32-an-invalidity-clear-poisons) records the source-established lowered-tree arithmetic order. Accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-12`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-12--division-by-zero-computations-poison-despite-a-cleared-delta) and [`SPEC-2026-07-21-05`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-21-05--runtime-invalid-integral-power-poisons-a-number-computation-target) record the shared computation-domain correction. No evidence or harness machinery was added.

## LF36 — numeric target storage separates decimal form, invalidity, and delta

> Date: 2026-07-19; amended 2026-07-21 and 2026-07-26. Sections: §5, §11. Basis: [`spec/09-computations.md` §§2–4](../spec/09-computations.md), pinned kernel `CalculationController`, `FormatDefinitionZahl`, `BigDecimalUtils`, computation-result, and calculation-cache source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits store implementation and IF34/IF82/IF92 findings at `3194c1f7`, red/green conformance, and trusted stored-number/target/delta laws.

A numeric expression value and a stored Number are different domains. Expression arithmetic stays an exact `Rat` with explicit rounding at semantic boundaries; target storage needs an exact decimal representation because `7` and `7.00` have equal rational amount but different change behavior. The smallest coherent stored type is therefore `{unscaled : Int, scale : Nat}`. Its plain rendering is derived rather than separately mutable, so coefficient, scale, amount, text, structural equality, and delta equality cannot drift apart.

The ordinary target path applies scale-19 `HALF_UP`, strips only fractional trailing zeros, and pads to at least `minFractionalDigits`.
