It does not shorten a fitting value to satisfy the universal 15-digit check: the full attempt survives as target rejection. The policy checks digit length before unsigned-negative output. Natural scale above `maxFractionalDigits` is not rounded away on that entry point, which remains fail-closed. The separate explicit `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` suppression path first produces the same scale-19 canonical decimal, retains it unchanged at 16 or fewer significant digits, and otherwise rounds that pre-rounded value to the greatest nonnegative fractional scale allowed by the 16-digit budget. This ordering matters at double-rounding boundaries. The resulting no-fit attempt is rejected unconditionally; a reduced digit or signedness failure supplies the more specific cause, otherwise the suppressed scale mismatch does. [`SPEC-2026-07-19-15`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-15--computed-number-storage-has-distinct-fit-and-warning-suppressed-no-fit-branches) carries the canonical correction and peer reconciliation.

The target result must preserve five meanings: clean no-result, accepted stored decimal, payloadful target rejection, calculation-local invalidity with no attempted decimal, and inherited operand poison. Division domain failure becomes the fourth case and carries the language-neutral `calculationValue` cause behind `berechnungsWertFehler`; it is not an ordinary target rejection and not an inherited source cause. Delta projection deliberately loses some of this information: accepted values compare coefficient and scale; rejection is unconditionally ERRORED; clean no-result, calculation invalidity, and poison are all silent over an empty prior and CLEARED over a filled prior. Trusted laws record both that equality and the non-equivalence it conceals, preparing the later dependent-read separator without inventing a scheduler.

Lean treatment: [`NumericStoredNumber.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericStoredNumber.lean) owns the exact decimal form, normalized dot-decimal rendering, scale-19 coefficient conversion, zero stripping, minimum-scale padding, and the separate significant-digit-bounded conversion. Its [proof module](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericStoredNumber.lean) proves that stripping and padding each preserve amount, that ordinary conversion preserves exactly the scale-19 `HALF_UP` result, and that bounded conversion either retains the canonical result or rounds that result at the derived scale; it also records the `7` versus `7.00` non-law. Its [conformance module](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericStoredNumber.lean) separates whole, leading-fractional-zero, negative, and zero rendering. [`NumericTarget.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericTarget.lean) separately owns the post-static-admission policy, ordinary fail-closed routing, explicit warning-suppressed no-fit routing, shared reduced attempt checks, five target outcomes, and placement-erasing delta projection. Its [proofs](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericTarget.lean) preserve domain/poison causes, ordinary no-fit refusal, digit-attempt retention, signed fit acceptance, suppression irrelevance on fit, unconditional no-fit rejection, specific-error precedence, exact stored-form delta laws, and semantic constructor distinctions; its [conformance cases](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericTarget.lean) distinguish pre-round-before-fit routing, the exact 15-digit boundary, digit-before-signedness precedence, full overflow, ordinary no-fit refusal, short and bounded suppressed no-fit, double-round staging, reduced-error precedence, domain/poison distinction, and delta behavior.

Limit: the complete ordinary target policy now retains numeric bounds, zero prohibition, explicit effective integer-digit capacity, rendered lengths, and the warning-suppression directive. Computed rendering still cannot reach the input-only leading-zero check, custom targets remain excluded, and exact V2 source object identity belongs to LF37 rather than this target-format policy. Repeatable targets, public protocol, and locally retained portable numeric-target observations remain open. a12-dmkits' multi-route differentials are design knowledge and triangulation, not this repository's retained evidence. No evidence, protocol, harness, governance, or dependency work was added.

## LF37 — numeric application preserves placement but erases cause provenance

> Date: 2026-07-19; amended 2026-07-26 and 2026-07-30. Sections: §1, §5, §11. Basis: [`spec/01-data-model.md` §4.3](../spec/01-data-model.md#43-the-compute--apply--validate-flow) and [`spec/09-computations.md` §§2–4](../spec/09-computations.md), pinned kernel V2 computation-result/application, `ValidationData`, `FormatDefinitionZahl`, and `NumberUtil` source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a12-dmkits IF126 plus the retained-result matrix at `f6dc22d24cc865c02c7f5b3beec5c0cff0bec140`, red/green conformance, and trusted relational/application laws.

Exact one-outcome application needs one state dimension that delta deliberately erased. An absent target and a present-empty target both project to the empty delta prior, yet applying clean no-value directly to that prior leaves the first absent and the second present-empty. A filled source additionally needs typed comparison identity: V2 `String` and `BigDecimal` values can produce the same internal text, and a negative-scale `BigDecimal` loses its scale when rendered, while result classification later uses object equality against canonical computed `BigDecimal`. `NumericSourceIdentity` therefore retains either an exact nonnegative-scale decimal or the quotient `nonComputedForm`, whose members can never equal computed Number output. An accepted result yields `presentValue (.decimal stored)`. At this direct transition boundary, every outcome with no applied value—clean no-result, target rejection, calculation invalidity, or inherited poison—empties either filled prior form in place and never creates an absent target solely to clear it.

Retained-result application is a distinct transition. Classification against the source first decides whether a CLEARED action exists; once retained, that action places present-empty in a separate destination even when the target and its addressed repeatable ancestry were absent. No-value over an absent source target mints no action, ERRORED retains its destination-presence guard, and changed VALUE places its exact decimal plus directly addressed ancestry. The direct `NumericTargetOutcome.noValue.applyTo absent = absent` equation and retained `absent.applyRetainedClear = presentEmpty` equation are therefore both correct at different boundaries. a12-dmkits `f6dc22d2` separates them across both kernel strategies and also observes row-2 creation; its route does not expose repetition count, so predecessor padding and row order remain unstated.

Final state is not semantic provenance. Quiet no-value and target rejection can both leave a formerly filled target present-empty, while their deltas remain CLEARED versus ERRORED. Quiet no-value and calculation invalidity go further: exact application and delta both agree for every prior state, yet the outcomes remain different because calculation invalidity, unlike quiet no-value, can poison a dependent read. A rejected attempt likewise remains on the full outcome and ERRORED delta but is never stored by application. These information-loss laws prevent a later consumer from reconstructing computation meaning from the document or delta alone.

Lean treatment: [`NumericApplication.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericApplication.lean) defines the Number-specific three-state placement, comparison-relevant source identity, direct outcome transition, and distinct retained-clear transition without refactoring unrelated target families into a generic framework. Its [proof module](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericApplication.lean) gives an independently enumerated direct relation, evaluator/relation equivalence, determinism, exact source-identity agreement, clearing and presence laws, the retained-clear law, and the direct-versus-retained non-law. Its [conformance module](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericApplication.lean) locks accepted `7.00` over absent, empty, decimal `7`, and equal decimal `7.00` states; all four no-applied outcome classes; absent-versus-present-empty loss; and unconditional ERRORED provenance. [`NumericComputation/SourceTarget.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericComputation/SourceTarget.lean) projects exact placement from the immutable checked document. [`NumericComputation/RunResult.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericComputation/RunResult.lean) pairs that source state with each rich run outcome before projecting all successes, their typed changed subset, payloadful errors, source-filled clears, and the unchanged residual channel. Its proofs lock changed-success inclusion, exact error-channel meaning, and extensional collection order. [`NumericComputation/RunApplication.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/NumericComputation/RunApplication.lean) consumes only clear, error, and change actions against a caller-supplied destination, rejects repeated action targets, and adds a destination-model-indexed projection for exact addresses. It reuses [`repeatableAncestorRowsFor`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/CheckedDocument.lean) to record only directly created absent ancestors and never converts that lower-bound observation into an invented predecessor or ordering claim. Conformance carries the six-row retained-action matrix, destination-address rejection, source/destination disagreement, unrelated-field preservation, and malformed duplicate actions; nested directly addressed ancestry is locked by the generic row-derivation law rather than mislabeled as a second kernel observation. The kernel applies the retained collections through one type-neutral computed-field-instance boundary, so the existing String, Time, Date, and DateTime whole-result owners now use the same action/outcome separation; their red/green absent-target cases lock that structural consequence while their direct outcome relations remain unchanged.

Limit: `NumericTargetState` records final placement and exactly the source-equality quotient needed by computed Number output; it does not preserve the raw lexical value inside `nonComputedForm`, because no current result or application consumer can distinguish those members. A typed-equal accepted result is not physically rewritten by the kernel because only changed results are applied, but this abstraction has the same final decimal state. The checked application result is a Number-cell and directly-created-ancestor projection indexed only by its checked destination model, not a complete materialized document ready for later validation. The retained view itself has no source-model index, so source/destination model compatibility is an explicit caller precondition; every action address is checked against the destination, but model identity and the kernel's response to incompatibility remain open. Predecessor padding, created-row order, repeatable application for non-Number families, and post-application validation also remain open. The upstream runtime matrix exercises one missing row of a repeatable Number target; deeper directly addressed ancestry and the other scalar-family absent-target locks are source-fidelity consequences of the generic application owner, not independent type-specific kernel observations. LF38 separately owns the pre-consumer dependency observation. [`EXP-2026-07-30-02`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#exp-2026-07-30-02--source-classified-clear-against-an-absent-destination) is accepted at `f6dc22d2`; this exact matrix is Kernel-locked upstream but not a project-local retained observation. The deprecated mutable V1 application route is not part of the supported account. [`SPEC-2026-07-26-05`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-26-05--number-result-change-detection-uses-source-v2-object-identity) carries the source-identity correction. No evidence, protocol, harness, governance, or dependency work was added.

## LF38 — numeric dependency meaning survives lossy application and delta

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §1, §5, §11. Basis: [`spec/09-computations.md` §§3.1–3.3 and §4](../spec/09-computations.md), pinned kernel `CalculationCommand`, `CalculationController`, `CalculationCache`, and generated calculation-alternative source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, red/green conformance, and trusted dependency-projection laws.

A completed Number target has a dependency meaning that cannot be reconstructed from either its applied document state or reported delta. Clean no-result supplies an ordinary empty dependency; accepted output supplies the exact target-rendered `StoredNumber`; target rejection, calculation-local invalidity, and inherited invalidity all supply a poisoned dependency. The invalid classes do not expose their attempted value or producer-side cause at this boundary: the kernel's calculation cache checks only invalid membership and throws before returning value or presence. Their full provenance remains on `NumericTargetOutcome`.

This makes the division-by-zero separator exact. Over a stale filled target, clean no-result and `invalidNoValue calculationValue` both report CLEARED and both apply as present-empty; over an absent target, both report nothing and preserve absence. Their dependency observations still differ as `empty` versus `poisoned`, and their checked-cell reads differ as clean empty versus the common cause-blind `.computedDependency` poison. The integrated executable witness evaluates the source-established, already-resolved `RoundAccounting([Source] / 0, 2)` shape to domain failure, classifies it as calculation invalidity at the target, and projects poison. It does not claim that Lean currently checks the concrete wrapper's authoring legality or target assignment-scale gate.

Lean treatment: [`NumericDependency.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/NumericDependency.lean) defines `empty | value StoredNumber | poisoned`, the five-row projection from the existing complete target outcome, and its well-formed checked-cell embedding. The embedding exposes accepted output as the exact rational amount and every invalid producer class as the shared `.computedDependency` marker without reconstructing an attempted value or cause. Its [proof module](../A12Kernel/Proofs/NumericDependency.lean) characterizes exactly which outcomes yield empty and exact stored value, proves the checked-cell representation and reads, locks invalid-class collapse, and gives the universal application-plus-delta non-law. Its [conformance module](../A12Kernel/Conformance/NumericDependency.lean) covers all producer classes, exact `7.00`, the complete resolved division route, stale clearing, fresh silence, and clean-empty versus invalid checked-cell reads.

Limit: this is a pre-consumer observation, not `CellObservation`, a synthetic checked cell, a downstream numeric-expression evaluator, a document overlay, or a scheduler. Existing Number read semantics establish that ordinary empty is consumed as zero, but this capsule does not yet inject the observation into `ScalarComputationContext` or prove multi-read order. It does not cover invalid power, precondition/table integration, transitive dependencies, repeats, target paths, message/path provenance, numeric `Abs`/`Min`/`Max` wrapper nodes, Date-shift projection, or project-local portable dual-route evidence. The canonical spec already states the behavior, and accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-12`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-12--division-by-zero-computations-poison-despite-a-cleared-delta) records a12-dmkits's numeric wrapper, Number-target/dependent-poison, and Date-shift controls at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`; that peer closure does not widen this Lean capsule or create retained local evidence. No evidence, protocol, harness, governance, dependency, or generic abstraction was added.

## LF39 — resolved whole-rule messages are a checked post-verdict boundary

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §1, §10, §12, §13. Basis: [`spec/01-data-model.md` §§4.1 and 4.3](../spec/01-data-model.md), [`spec/08-paths-and-references.md`](../spec/08-paths-and-references.md), [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md), [`spec/11-messages-and-custom.md`](../spec/11-messages-and-custom.md), red/green conformance, trusted rule-emission laws, and independent source/spec/proof review.

A whole validation rule does not author its VALUE/OMISSION message type. Its condition first yields the complete `Verdict`; only `fired polarity` creates a message, while `notFired` and `unknown` remain distinct internally even though both emit nothing. The emitted message copies an explicit error address, error code, severity, and already-resolved text and derives its message type from that firing polarity. Changing the error field, code, severity, or resolved text therefore cannot change condition truth or polarity. ERROR, WARNING, and INFO all emit when the same condition fires, but only ERROR makes the document invalid.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/ValidationRule.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ValidationRule.lean) defines `ResolvedFlatRule`, `FlatRuleOutcome`, and `FlatRuleMessage` as the low-level already-lowered, per-instance emission boundary. [`Elaboration/ValidationRule.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/ValidationRule.lean) accepts an existing `CheckedFlatCondition`, resolves the explicit error-field ID uniquely in the same `FlatModel`, requires it to be nonrepeatable and referenced by the condition, and evaluates `RawFlatContext` only after that model derives the checked cells. It never infers the error field from the first reference. `hasContent` remains an authoritative caller input because model checking validates cells but does not derive row content or topology. [`Proofs/ValidationRule.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/ValidationRule.lean) proves verdict recovery, exact core and checked message transport, metadata independence, ERROR-only invalidation, assembly coherence, and the equal-silence/non-equal-verdict boundary. [`Conformance/ValidationRule.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/ValidationRule.lean) separates empty OMISSION from filled VALUE, false from malformed silence, the row gate, all severities, an explicit non-first error field, repeatable/unreferenced/unknown field rejection, and token-looking text that remains byte-for-byte opaque.

`ResolvedMessageText` is intentionally not an authored template and this capsule implements no interpolation. Its empty repetition path is valid only because repeatable error fields are rejected. Rule path, referenced-field metadata, fill-to-fix metadata, partial validation, rule collections and ordering, generated checks, labels, localization, custom conditions, concrete syntax, and public protocol exposure remain outside. The one-constructor mandatory severity and message-type types stay as stronger generated-rule refinements until an actual generated-rule integration consumes the shared boundary. Existing retained validation evidence anchors component polarity and selected pointers, not checked rule assembly, code/severity independence, resolved text, or the hidden silent distinction, so the whole capsule remains `external evidence pending`. The generated-computation validation audit exposed the missing code channel before its capsule consumed this boundary; the error code is now transported by the general rule boundary rather than a computation-specific message type. The canonical spec already requires error codes, and accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-06`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-06--first-match-computation-differs-from-all-alternatives-validation) closes the peer selection-versus-generated-validation mechanism at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`; generated-rule message-text transport remains a separate peer concern rather than a duplicate semantic-ledger row. No support, evidence, harness, governance, protocol, or dependency code was added.

## LF40 — computation selects one alternative while generated validation retains every alternative

> Date: 2026-07-19; amended 2026-07-21. Sections: §1, §11, §12. Basis: [`spec/01-data-model.md` §3.2](../spec/01-data-model.md), [`spec/09-computations.md` §§5–6](../spec/09-computations.md), [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md), pinned kernel `IComputation`, `ValidationModelConverter`, `ComputationAlternativesJoiner`, normalized-calculation builder, and Java/Groovy/JavaScript alternative templates at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, a12-dmkits read-only triangulation through its declaration-ordered list reduction, red/green conformance, and trusted structural/gating laws.

A computation alternative table has two deliberately different consumers. Computation scans in declaration order and selects the first guard that is known true; a selected operation terminates the scan. The generated validation rule instead retains every alternative as a guarded mismatch. Therefore, with the same holding guard on literal operations `1` then `2`, a stored target `1` is exactly the first selected computation result and still produces a generated error because the later retained mismatch is true. Equal results or a nonholding later guard are silent controls. Mutual exclusivity is author discipline, not a premise supplied by the runtime.

Shared guard syntax does not imply a shared evaluator. Computation observes checked cells in computation phase and preserves a read poison; generated validation is an ordinary validation condition with unknown, strong-Kleene connective behavior, and data-derived VALUE/OMISSION polarity. In the separating case `poisoned FieldFilled Or healthy FieldFilled`, computation aborts at the left read while generated validation can still fire because the healthy validation branch dominates unknown. Conversely, an empty `FieldNotFilled` guard can make the generated ERROR message an OMISSION. Any future agreement theorem therefore needs explicit phase-compatible hypotheses in addition to mutual exclusivity or equal results.

Lean treatment: [`Elaboration/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean) admits a structurally nonempty table of decoded numeric literals for one nonrepeatable Number target. An explicit sum separates one optionally guarded alternative from a two-leading-row guarded table with one ordered remainder; no synthetic true condition stands in for an absent singleton guard. It reuses `ComputationAlternative.selectFirst` after erasing validation-only tolerance metadata, structurally translates each present direct scalar-presence/`And`/`Or` guard into `FlatCondition`, and lowers the singleton comparison or declaration-ordered wider mismatch fold below an explicit `FieldFilled(target)` gate. Each alternative independently lowers to strict `!=` or its optional fixed tolerance band through the shared `NumericValidationOp`; only the strict branch applies exact-scale admission. Number/Boolean/Confirm/String guard declarations and the Number target are checked against the same validated model. The common and every present alternative guard reject nested references to that target before translation, while `CheckedResolvedFlatRule` supplies target address, computation name as error code, fixed ERROR severity, resolved text, and verdict-derived message polarity. [`Proofs/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean) locks universal guard self-reference rejection, singleton and guarded-first selection, per-alternative tolerance erasure, exact minimum guarded shape, append-at-right order, declaration-order recovery, and universal empty-target suppression. [`Conformance/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/GeneratedComputationValidation.lean) separates absent, nonholding own, and nonholding common singleton guards; singleton scale/tolerance admission; common/first/third/singleton self-reference origins; and a reached third-alternative separator and third-position scale diagnostic alongside the common/overlap, target-gating, phase, polarity, guard-kind, and rejection matrix.

This remains a literal table fragment, not general generated-computation validation. It assumes the source computation has passed wider authoring legality; zero/default rejection belongs to that wider boundary. The separate checked plain numeric-operation capsule now rejects operation-side target self-reference, but it is not yet integrated into this table or ordinary generated-rule condition because that would require a shared checked numeric-expression leaf rather than a parallel condition mechanism. Operation no-value/domain failure, warning-suppressed assignment legality, runtime target checks, repeatable evaluation, anchoring, scheduling, application, partial validation, concrete syntax, protocol, and portable observations remain outside. The source audit confirms the mechanism but supplies no retained black-box observation, so the capsule remains `external evidence pending`. a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` locks the distinct first-match computation and all-alternatives validation mechanisms under accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-06`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-06--first-match-computation-differs-from-all-alternatives-validation); revisions `d6a08f5b53022d9caae35e6d842a2b7b22cd5b25` and `7b39728eafdb53b0a69fcafd34817980bb900632` preserve the per-alternative tolerance path under accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-19-13`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-19-13--tolerance-normalizes-operands-first-and-uses-directional-inequality-polarity). The newly canonicalized target-reference rule is queued under [`SPEC-2026-07-21-06`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-21-06--computations-reject-references-to-their-own-target). No evidence, protocol, harness, governance, scheduler, parallel table framework, or dependency work was added.

## LF41 — calendar reality and A12 Date value admission are different types

> Date: 2026-07-19. Sections: §6, §12. Basis: the decoded chronology, calendar-reality, and value-floor clauses of [`spec/05-dates-and-time.md` §§1, 3–4](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md), pinned kernel format, strict civil parser, universal-floor, and direct-comparison source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits Date implementation and focused differentials at revision `e346e968e80ebcd596f14660db19b03dcacada54`, red/green conformance including a wrong componentwise-order mutation, and trusted constructor/order laws.

A decoded year/month/day triple, a real calendar day, and an A12-storable Date are three different states. Calendar construction uses positive-era proleptic-Gregorian month lengths and leap years; A12 stored and computed full-Date values additionally require the inclusive universal floor 1583-10-16. The floor is not calendar reality: `1500-01-01` and `1583-10-15` are real days but not admitted values. The optional 1900 field policy is different again, so `1899-12-31` passes the universal floor.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/FullDate.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/FullDate.lean) represents the stages as raw `DateParts`, proof-carrying `CivilDate`, and floor-certified `FullDate`. Both constructors are partial and fail closed. Strict `before` compares the admitted components lexicographically; it does not encode dates as an epoch count or compare all components conjunctively. Verdict and polarity are deferred until a later operand layer can carry empty/formal/not-given provenance. [`Proofs/FullDate.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/FullDate.lean) exposes the leap-aware reality invariant, characterizes both constructors, carries reality/floor certificates, proves total strict chronology, and connects executable comparison to that order. [`Conformance/FullDate.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/FullDate.lean) locks every ordinary-month length, Gregorian century rules, impossible dates, positive-era bounds, both floor sides, the 1899 separator, year/month/day crossings, equality/reversal, and the componentwise-order non-law.

Limit: this begins after component decoding and deliberately defines an unbounded positive-era Gregorian account plus strict `<`; year zero and negative years are rejected by the Lean constructor, while exact parser range and kernel correspondence for values outside the reachable full-Date fragment remain unclaimed. It does not claim textual formats, optional-1900 policy integration, `Value`/`CheckedCell`/formal-error or empty semantics, polarity, the other five operators, `Date(...)`, fragments/ranges/Base Year, DateTime/Time, zones/DST/`Today`/`World`, arithmetic/differences, checked rule lowering, or protocol support. Pinned source and a12-dmkits tests ground the chosen reachable account; this repository retains no portable Date observation, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`. The canonical spec already distinguishes these clauses, so no `spec/` correction or new synchronization-ledger row was warranted. No support, evidence, protocol, harness, governance, dependency, or general temporal framework was added.

## LF42 — DateTime arithmetic needs scalar instant identity apart from wall labels

> Date: 2026-07-19; extended 2026-07-22. Sections: §1, §6, §12. Basis: the decoded whole-second, exact validation-clock, value-floor, UTC, model-zone overlap/gap, instant-arithmetic, and stateful calendar-day clauses of [`spec/05-dates-and-time.md` §§2–5, 7, and 9](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md); pinned kernel canonical-format, strict model-zone parsing, validation-clock injection, numeric conversion, calendar-add, Date-equality/order, epoch-millisecond difference, and day-difference source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; read-only a12-dmkits `DateMath`/`ExprEval` triangulation plus maintained DateTime, DST, and calendar-step differentials; wrong-unit, wrong-overlap-policy, floor-division, wall-coordinate, same-second comparison, and sub-second quotient red/green separators; independent semantic, Lean-proof, and architecture reviews; trusted constructor, resolver-branch, same-date, shift-algebra, resolved-difference, and signed calendar-day laws.

A local DateTime such as `2024-10-27T02:30:00` is a wall label; arithmetic and comparison operate on an instant. UTC deterministically resolves the label because it has no gaps, folds, or offset, but those concepts must remain different types: under Berlin the autumn label can be rendered from two different instants, and reparsing it chooses the standard-time one. Chained `AddHours` must retain the arithmetic instant rather than round-trip through a local string.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/CivilDateCoordinate.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/CivilDateCoordinate.lean) owns the executable Gregorian day coordinate, its bounded inverse, and checked successor. [`Core.lean`](../A12Kernel/Core.lean) owns the shared exact `{ epochMillis : Int }` `Instant`; decoded whole-second `TimeOfDay` and `LocalDateTime` labels remain separate from that identity. [`Semantics/DateTime.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateTime.lean) resolves UTC labels, decodes an exact instant after a profile selects its offset, and preserves exact identity through shifts. `LocalDateTime.Before` orders admitted wall labels before zone resolution. [`Semantics/BerlinLegacyTimeZone.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/BerlinLegacyTimeZone.lean) implements the named 62-transition legacy profile plus post-1997 recurrence and resolves fresh labels by testing its three historical candidate offsets against that same oracle. Gaps therefore have no candidate; overlaps choose the first valid ascending offset, which is the smaller/after offset even for CEMT. [`Semantics/DateTimeDifference.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateTimeDifference.lean) closes the post-resolution `DifferenceInHours/Minutes/Seconds` core through `(second.epochMillis − first.epochMillis).tdiv (unitSeconds * 1000)`. [`Semantics/BerlinLegacyCalendarArithmetic.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/BerlinLegacyCalendarArithmetic.lean) owns source-offset day mutation, sign-independent month/year compute-time resolution, and completed month/year qualification. [`Semantics/DateTimeDayDifference.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateTimeDayDifference.lean) separately counts stateful Berlin calendar landings across the complete pinned profile and dispatches UTC without projecting Berlin onto another zone. Trusted laws establish sub-day unit positivity, self-zero, swap negation, millisecond-aware seconds, exact-unit recovery, smaller-offset priority, zero-mutation identity, day-target date preservation, completed-period sign symmetry, and general Berlin day self-zero and swap negation. Cases lock same-second exact comparison, `999 ms`/`1000 ms` boundaries, historical-table and recurrence boundaries, CEMT, modern and historical gap/overlap classes, long landings whose source offset separates the mechanism from direction, target-date fallback, exact milliseconds, the day/year landing non-law, the fresh-source month search, elapsed-seconds non-equivalence, long-range stepping, UTC, and fail-closed unsupported-zone use.

Limit: this remains a selected post-parse fragment: exact instant identity and resolved sub-day differences, general UTC, a complete pinned Berlin offset/fresh-label profile, and post-floor calendar arithmetic for those two concrete profiles. The successor and order theorems close the internal coordinate bridge for modeled positive-era Gregorian labels, but they do not establish parser reachability, external correspondence, another model zone, pre-floor hybrid-calendar identity, or a general zone-sensitive order. Later checked flat-temporal capsules connect exact `Now` to direct comparison, typed temporal `Value`/field admission, static component gates, `Today`, wider injected model-zone dispatch, bounded Time computation/application, and FULL Date target evaluation; partial Date computation targets are now explicitly refused. Wider temporal targets, parser/format breadth, recursive or wider temporal arithmetic, other zones, gap-to-formal-error projection, wider `Today`/`Now` consumers, and protocol support remain open. Maintained differentials and pinned source establish the selected discriminators, the a12-dmkits IF155 handback establishes the complete named profile, accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-22-02`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-22-02--now-retains-exact-epoch-millisecond-identity) records revision `9c8da06e` for exact milliseconds, and revisions `456dca65`/`0621f088` independently lock the FULL-only Date target gate and computed-Time identity/result boundary. This repository still retains no portable DateTime observation. No support, evidence, protocol, harness, new governance mechanism, dependency, or generic temporal framework was added.

## LF43 — checked message authoring lowers to one-pass display-layer rendering

> Date: 2026-07-19; corrected and extended 2026-07-21 and 2026-07-30. Sections: §3, §13. Basis: [`spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md`](../spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md), [`spec/11-messages-and-custom.md`](../spec/11-messages-and-custom.md), pinned kernel static checking, rule-template lowering, substitution, public display-data, label-resolution, and format-default source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; read-only a12-dmkits renderer and filled/empty interpolation differentials at revision `8b18848de798da0d24158f8438b81c3eb53c3eb2`, followed by the reviewed provider/default/raw-display implementation and dual-route controls at revision `20230e403fa085c782534025f890669a975999a8`; red/green conformance and trusted authoring/reference/provider/default/opacity/order/post-fire laws.

The kernel performs rule-message interpolation as one ordered split/substitution/join pass over authored `$...$` text. Lean starts after that syntax has been decoded and field-resolved: it renders structured parts from left to right, treats an escaped dollar as ordinary text, and never parses replacement bytes again. A display value such as `$Other$`, HTML-sensitive text, or CRLF therefore remains opaque once supplied to this renderer. The public validation route obtains that value from caller/display data, not from the normalized evaluation cache. Maintained a12-dmkits differentials now confirm through both kernel strategies that an ordinary String can be CRLF-normalized for condition evaluation while its message display still carries raw CRLF.

One-pass rendering does not imply one authored grammar. a12-dmkits revision `ed0b0cd9f7110daa354c2404e7b9ca6df4f42fa3` establishes that an `en_US` String-pattern field message accepts only the fixed lowercase tokens `$field$` and `$field.value$`; it rejects rule-style `$$` as an empty parameter and rejects both `$Field$` and an actual name such as `$Code$`. Its value insertion is still one-pass and opaque. The measured requiredness producer accepts the same doubled-dollar shape and preserves `$$`, so even two field-owned producers cannot share a parser merely because their resolved parts can share a renderer.

Field display names and empty values are likewise external display-policy inputs, not intrinsic `Value` rendering. The public service first accepts an effective provider result exactly, including an empty string; otherwise it uses a nonempty localized model label and finally the identifier's debug representation. A missing or empty `getValueForDisplay` result selects the field format's exact default. The current modern `DocumentV2` Number default follows minimum fractional digits—`0`, `0.00`, and so on—and the reviewed probes returned dot-decimal `0.00` under both US and German locales and under a comma `modelConfig.decimalSeparator`; locale-derived comma output was refuted. String supplies empty. Present Number, Date, Boolean, Confirm, enumeration, and String display forms remain caller/format results, and the renderer adds no unit or second formatting pass. A distinct legacy or presentation-information route remains outside until characterized.

Lean treatment: [`ValidationMessageAuthoring.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/ValidationMessageAuthoring.lean) owns one shared nonempty printable-ASCII and delimiter-balance gate plus the rule producer's parameter parser, and [`StringPatternMessage.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/StringPatternMessage.lean) consumes that gate for the `en_US` String-pattern producer's own parser. The two producers share the gate and the refusal type and nothing else, which is why they are separate modules. The rule parser decodes each `$$` pair, resolves bare nonrepeatable fields in the condition group, and retains condition-membership proofs. The String-pattern parser admits only the two measured fixed lowercase tokens, rejects its empty `$$` parameter, and lowers caller-selected owning-field name/value bytes directly as opaque text rather than applying rule-path lookup or empty-display defaults. Both lower to the existing `MessageRenderPlan`, so no raw delimiter survives to reinterpret replacements. `RenderedStringPatternError` attaches that resolved text while retaining the established `.pattern` error. [`Semantics/ValidationRule.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ValidationRule.lean) remains the sole owner of `MessageRenderPart`, `MessageRenderPlan`, and `ResolvedMessageText`. The proof module locks rule condition membership, opacity, and verdict independence plus field-value opacity and preservation of the pattern error. Conformance separates rule-dollar decoding from field-dollar rejection, fixed name/value tokens from stylized and actual names, malformed delimiters, and token-looking replacement bytes.

Limit: the checked raw authoring fragment deliberately excludes non-ASCII text, rule quoting/paths/stars/repetition/index/category/semantic-index/`BaseYear`, field locales other than the measured `en_US` String-pattern producer, complete requiredness grammar, other field-type producers, locale/provider invocation, document-value lookup, empty-value fallback, exact-default derivation, and debug-path construction. `ValidationMessageInputs` and `StringPatternMessageInputs` begin after their different display-policy effects and do not claim that provider invocation itself is deferred. The canonical spec states the wider syntax and display cascade, while the bounded modern `DocumentV2` dot-default profile is accepted under [`SPEC-2026-07-21-04`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-21-04--message-format-default-follows-the-actual-documentv2-profile). a12-dmkits revision `351bf9f2` still leaves rule-owned `$$` doubled because its regex recognizes only nonempty tokens; that peer implementation gap remains under SG10 and is independent of the now-locked field behavior. No support, evidence, protocol, harness, new governance mechanism, dependency, or general formatting framework was added.

## LF44 — `No` and `NotAll` value-list quantifiers are not duals

> Date: 2026-07-20; corrected 2026-07-23. Sections: §3, §5, §8, §12. Basis: [`spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md` §B.3](../spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md#b3-the-value-list-quantifiers--per-cell-three-way-classification-), pinned kernel 30.8.1 value-list materialization and quantifier methods at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`, read-only a12-dmkits [`ValueListQuantEval.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/eval/ValueListQuantEval.kt) and maintained value-list differentials through revision `5350fa17f8bd9721d75039d07a88ab90f5e68d7e`; red/green ordered separators and trusted scan laws.

Both sides are classified per cell, but no single global side fold describes all three operators. `AtLeastOne` and `No` materialize values before fields; `NotAll` first checks for any present field, materializes values only when that prerequisite holds, and then restarts its fields scan. Values-side UNKNOWN therefore poisons `No` before an immediate fields match and poisons `NotAll` only after a present field exists. Fields-side `No` is encounter-ordered: `[present "A", unknown]` against member `"A"` is definitely not fired, while reversing those cells is UNKNOWN. `NotAll` skips fields-side UNKNOWN and fires at the first present outside witness.

Polarity is also encounter-sensitive. `No` accumulates empty/tail/filter omission only along the reached absence scan. `AtLeastOne` and `NotAll` use the matching or outside-witness fields operand, so an earlier filtered nonwitness does not taint a later unfiltered witness. On the values side, `AtLeastOne` retains a filter only when that operand contributed a present selected member; the poison-sensitive `No`/`NotAll` materialization retains reached filter, empty, and tail potential. Empty members still contribute no value: an empty Number member never matches zero.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/ValueList.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/ValueList.lean) keeps the kind-indexed atom domain and existing classified partial-validation boundary, adds one reusable fields-side `No` scan, and evaluates full-validation authored operand lists through `ValueListQuantifier.evalOrdered`. The three operator arms remain separate because their pass structure genuinely differs. Proofs lock match-before-UNKNOWN, UNKNOWN-before-match, values-before-fields poison, the `NotAll` no-present prepass, and reached-filter omission; conformance cases lock witness-specific fields polarity and selected-present values polarity.

Limit: this finding closes ordered resolved full-validation semantics. The later checked addressed construction now supplies Number and token operands with model certificates, environments, structural failures, and positional partial relevance; unsupported kinds, protocol exposure, and project-local portable observations remain open. Correspondence remains project-local `external evidence pending`; accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-23-16`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-23-16--value-list-quantifiers-preserve-operator-specific-encounter-order) and a12-dmkits revision `7a723254` triangulate the three pass structures and witness-specific polarity, with the explicit qualification that terminal `notFired` versus `unknown` is not independently message-observable. No support mechanism, evidence machinery, protocol, dependency, or generic quantifier framework was added.

## LF45 — repetition uniqueness builds one branch-independent duplicate relation before verdict composition

> Date: 2026-07-20; extended with checked Enumeration authoring on 2026-07-22 and nested whole-rule scope on 2026-07-26. Sections: §3, §5, §9, §12. Basis: [`spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §6](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#6-repetitionnotunique-precisely-), pinned kernel 30.8.1 parser `CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl`, code-generation `RepetitionNotUniqueCreator`, runtime `RuntimeController.wiederholungNichtEindeutig`/`ermittleWiederholungenMitDuplikaten`/`getWerteOrNullIfEinFeldNichtPruefungsRelevant`, and `ValidierungsErgebnis`, read-only a12-dmkits [`RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator.kt) plus maintained `RepetitionNotUnique*DiffTest` families including [`RepetitionNotUniqueNestedDiffTest.kt`](../../a12-rulekit/adapter/src/test/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/adapter/laws/RepetitionNotUniqueNestedDiffTest.kt) at reviewed revisions `07fb8c03f5519950567c726372568769ad06beb4` and `83dd514f9283b9f62dbe6ee6f238e5c67a00e9c6`; red/green separating cases, an optional-empty equality mutation, and trusted duplicate-membership, firing, cluster, classification, checked-projection, depth-admission, unique-identity, and distinct-peer laws.

`RepetitionNotUnique` is not a guarded search. This capsule consumes already-classified candidate key rows from the complete selected scope, excludes a row when any key cell is formally invalid, skips an all-empty tuple, and groups the remaining rows by exact typed composite key. Each row is identified by its complete repetition `Env`; an inner row numbered 1 beneath one outer row is not the same identity as inner row 1 beneath another. Optional empty components are valid tuple members and match optional empties; they make a duplicate OMISSION-typed. Fully present duplicates are VALUE-typed. Number keys compare through the kernel's scale-19 normalized value boundary, canonical token keys compare exactly, and component position and tuple length matter. Whole-scope evaluation retains each firing row's complete peer cluster in supplied scope order; that order is an internal deterministic representation, not a claim about future pointer order. The later checked-authoring audit exposed that “canonical token” has a strict source boundary: parser admission is every direct value-validating field kind, but runtime special-cases only Number and compares the exact internally stored text for every other kind. The runtime's Number/String-only Javadoc is stale. Direct Enumeration RNU therefore uses the checked stored token and cannot reuse a category projection; temporal RNU cannot reuse ordinary instant equality.

Only after that relation exists does the current row's leaf verdict enter the ordinary `And`/`Or` algebra. A guard-false peer still makes another row a duplicate even though the guarded row emits no message; deleting that peer therefore changes the remaining row's RNU truth. This is the central non-law separating RNU from a branch-local query or message-side recognizer.

The internal false/unknown boundary needs one explicit assurance caveat. Kernel 30.8.1 first observes a formally invalid key as three-valued UNKNOWN, then drops that row, and finally represents both “not a duplicate” and “not check-relevant” as one `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN` result. The RNU leaf result and authored-message projection do not restore which silent verdict it was; separate formal diagnostics are orthogonal. Lean preserves the pre-collapse classification by mapping an invalid current key to `Verdict.unknown`, while a clean unique or all-empty row is `notFired`; a checked conformance case proves the internal distinction and their equal no-message projection. This is a chosen formal refinement, not a claim that external kernel output reveals a hidden UNKNOWN. a12-dmkits currently maps the missing RNU cache entry to leaf false and reconstructs whole-rule suppression separately, which remains observationally compatible.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/RepetitionNotUnique.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/RepetitionNotUnique.lean) owns typed key components, eligibility, exact tuple equality, the direct ordered duplicate-cluster representation, and per-row results. [`Elaboration/RepetitionNotUnique.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/RepetitionNotUnique.lean) constructs one Number/String/stored-Enumeration key sum over the shared nested topology and delegates Enumeration domain checking and token projection to the existing [`EnumerationRepetitionNotUnique`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/EnumerationRepetitionNotUnique.lean) bridge at each key's own environment prefix. [`ValidationRule.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/ValidationRule.lean) derives distinct bound prefixes through the existing star path and evaluates the source once per resulting default or authored-`@From` scope before ordinary connective evaluation. [`Proofs/RepetitionNotUnique.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/RepetitionNotUnique.lean) characterizes numeric equality, exact cluster membership, the refined UNKNOWN and all-empty boundaries, firing iff cluster cardinality is at least two, complete cluster exposure, uniqueness of complete identities, and a distinct matching peer for every well-formed in-scope firing. The validation-condition laws establish depth-independent full and partial admission, and the elaboration proof locks exact checked Enumeration delegation. [`Conformance/RepetitionNotUnique.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/RepetitionNotUnique.lean) separates three-row clusters, equal local indices beneath different outer rows, optional-empty polarity, all-empty skipping, invalid exclusion, scale-19 Number equality versus token identity, positional tuples, branch-independent composition, and the externally collapsed silence boundary. The checked source suite additionally separates stored Enumeration identity from category collisions, out-of-domain exclusion, and mixed Enumeration/Number tuples; the whole-rule suite separates global cross-parent duplicates, per-parent `@From`, ancestor-key projection, missing outer bindings, nested partial relevance, and immutable rule-row order.

Limit: the checked nested route implements default/explicit `@From`, all-component partial relevance, ordinary full/partial whole-rule placement, exact error addresses, and Number/String/direct ordinary-Enumeration keys along one branch. Remaining negative/filter/parallel legality, raw-text key kinds, prepared custom String checking, `referenced`/`fillToFix` peer pointers, protocol exposure, and any optimized cache remain outside. Current `RawCell` parsing loses the kernel's exact stored-text identity for temporal and potentially spelling-sensitive Boolean/Confirm keys, so those cannot be derived from semantic values. Pinned source and maintained dual-route differentials are sufficient to choose the base relation, nested scope partition, and visible Number/String outcomes, while the wider-kind contract is source-established and pending external separators under [`SPEC-2026-07-22-10`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-22-10--repetitionnotunique-compares-every-non-number-key-by-stored-text). This repository retains no portable RNU observation, and invalid-versus-unique hidden truth is intrinsically collapsed by the kernel API. Correspondence remains `external evidence pending`; no support mechanism, evidence machinery, protocol, dependency, or generic uniqueness framework was added.

## LF46 — constructed-Date validity needs a reason-bearing non-value

> Date: 2026-07-20; extended with the direct numeric consumer on 2026-07-20. Sections: §3, §6, §12. Basis: the corrected construction and validity clauses of [`spec/05-dates-and-time.md` §3](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#3-constructing-dates-and-checking-validity); pinned kernel 30.8.1 construction, `Valid`/`Invalid`, strict-calendar, authoring, and empty-row-eligibility source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; read-only a12-dmkits `DateStatus` triangulation and maintained construction/calendar consumer controls accepted at revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`; red/green conformance plus classification-precedence, verdict-polarity, selector-routing, incomplete-fixed, unreal-fillable, unavailable-as-zero, and comparison-truth-bypass mutations; independent semantic and Lean reviews; trusted availability, verdict-characterization, truth-complement, and non-value-reason laws.

Resolved three-part Date construction has four outcomes, not two. A complete triple that the calendar resolver accepts carries its `DateParts`; a complete rejected triple is unreal; at least one empty component with no malformed component is incomplete; and any formally unavailable component makes the construction unknown. Formal unavailability dominates emptiness. `Valid` fires VALUE only for the real case. `Invalid` does not merely test “not valid”: it stays silent for a real date, fires OMISSION for incomplete input, fires VALUE for a complete unreal date, and is UNKNOWN for formal unavailability.

This corrects one easy-to-miss distinction in the prose. At truth projection, `Valid` and `Invalid` are exact strong-Kleene complements, including the UNKNOWN/UNKNOWN arm. They are not mutually derivable as full A12 verdicts: incomplete and unreal both map to `Valid = notFired`, while `Invalid` must recover different polarities. A bare optional date therefore loses information required by a later authored message.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/DateConstruction.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateConstruction.lean) accepts only checked component availability plus a separate real/unreal decision for an all-present triple. It returns `real DateParts | incomplete | unreal | unknown`; the separate resolver is authoritative for calendar reality, so this layer neither substitutes the zone-free proleptic `CivilDate` predicate nor applies the later 1583 stored/computed-value floor. [`Proofs/DateConstruction.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/DateConstruction.lean) characterizes incomplete and UNKNOWN classification, exact retention of supplied reality, each fired projection, truth complementation, and the nearest non-law: `Valid(incomplete) = Valid(unreal)` while `Invalid(incomplete) ≠ Invalid(unreal)`. [`Conformance/DateConstruction.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/DateConstruction.lean) separates supplied real/unreal outcomes, all three empty positions, all-empty, supplied unreal behind emptiness, and unknown-plus-empty precedence. [`Semantics/DateConstructionNumeric.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateConstructionNumeric.lean) is the first explicit consumer: its closed day/month/year selector reads supplied real parts directly, maps incomplete and unreal to equal amount zero with `notGiven = true` versus `false`, and keeps construction UNKNOWN as cause-free unavailability rather than inventing `.malformed`. The comparison seam maps Date not-given to symmetric numeric fillability, so every true fixed-literal comparison over incomplete zero is OMISSION while the same true fixed-literal comparison over unreal zero is VALUE. [`Proofs/DateConstructionNumeric.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/DateConstructionNumeric.lean) proves exact selection, same-zero/distinct-provenance, universal unavailable-to-UNKNOWN, and true-comparison polarity for all six fixed-right operators; [`Conformance/DateConstructionNumeric.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/DateConstructionNumeric.lean) locks all real selectors, all non-value states, one false comparison, equality polarity, and both `< 1` and `>= 0` so an accidental grow-only encoding fails.

Limit: the capsule begins after authoring, kind checking, integer conversion, component availability, and calendar reality resolution. It does not retain exact formal causes or implement raw/checked cells, two- or four-part forms, Base Year, constant-only validity rejection, exact component bounds, row orchestration, target admission, checked lowering, or protocol exposure. Kernel source and maintained differentials establish the four classifications and selected outcomes, but only `Invalid` malformed handling is directly differentiated; malformed `Valid` and malformed-plus-empty precedence are source-established. The maintained all-empty differential fills an unrelated time field and therefore does not independently isolate a truly content-empty row; the kernel's explicit eligibility visitor establishes that gate. The base result deliberately remains `real DateParts | incomplete | unreal | unknown`; checked constructed-Date shift and difference consumers now combine it with their selected concrete profile and retain or use the exact model-zone instant without widening that reason carrier. Recursive composition, DateTime construction, direct Date `Min`/`Max`, other profiles, and target storage remain open. Reusing the zone-free proleptic `CivilDate` or existing resolved DateTime types for those consumers would be unsound: kernel 30.8.1 rejects the skipped `Pacific/Apia` date 2011-12-30 and accepts UTC 1500-02-29 under the Julian side of the cutover, while `CivilDate` gives the opposite classifications. This repository retains no portable Date observation, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`. a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` accepted both the truth-projection correction under [`SPEC-2026-07-20-06`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-20-06--constructed-date-validity-complements-only-at-truth-projection) and the constructor-specific reason/calendar consumer matrix under [`SPEC-2026-07-20-07`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-20-07--constructed-date-consumers-retain-reason-and-legacy-calendar-identity). No support, evidence, protocol, harness, governance mechanism, dependency, or calendar framework was added.

## LF47 — DifferenceInDays needs a model-zone calendar-step account

> Date: 2026-07-20; extended 2026-07-30. Sections: §6. Basis: [`spec/05-dates-and-time.md` §9](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#9-datetime-rides-the-date-format-definition--but-the-operand-gates-are-per-operator-); pinned kernel `BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDatumsDiff`/`getDifferenzInTagen` source at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; read-only a12-dmkits wall-day prose, evaluator, and focused modern-zone differentials at revision `0f797f17826380ce84b4279cc63a882f9d770717`; accepted peer reconciliation at revisions `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889`, `5716a964794e7b6e2e102ef56d898c5524f1d16a`, `17f95bcc7f0bd86b44413f1f41c5d2d351d84951`, `38b5046bc0c6cc26fcc4982dcb292b7b7822a981`, and `416065669ad177900e99e1dbec6cb53f3277252e`; independently reproduced JDK skipped-date and long-range calendar-field separators; and the general pinned-Berlin red/green Lean capsule.

The tempting pure account mapped every admitted Date/DateTime wall label to `prolepticDay × 86400 + timeOfDay` and divided the signed difference by 86,400. That account explains the maintained UTC and Berlin cases, including `23:00 → 01:00` next day yielding zero and an equal-wall-time spring-transition span yielding one. It is not the kernel's general mechanism. The runtime converts both operands to legacy calendars in the model zone, makes a whole-year lower-bound jump, and counts subsequent `Calendar.DAY_OF_MONTH` additions until the next landing would pass the later operand. The difference is visible even inside the supported Berlin profile: adding one calendar day to 2024-03-30T02:30 lands at 2024-03-31T01:30, before a 01:45 endpoint, so the kernel algorithm counts one while the 23 h 15 m wall-label quotient returns zero. A whole-date discontinuity supplies a second instance: in `Pacific/Apia`, adding one calendar day to 2011-12-29T12:00 lands on 2011-12-31T12:00, while the proleptic label distance is two dates.

Lean consequence: the selected day-difference capsule takes the narrower faithful route. The versioned Berlin profile resolves fresh operands. `BerlinLegacyCalendarArithmetic` owns the three calendar field mutations plus completed month/year qualification; `DateTimeDayDifference` owns only the single `365 × whole-years` lower-bound jump, residual scan, and concrete-profile dispatch. Each day mutation carries the exact source instant's offset and millisecond into the nominal target, re-fits to the actual target offset only when the re-fit preserves the selected civil date, and then retains the decoded clock and instant for the next step. Travel direction selects the target date but is not the landing discriminator. Month/year mutation instead uses sign-independent compute-time resolution plus the operation-specific February corrections. Completed months must search fresh source-relative candidates from the completed-year lower bound: a single final coordinate landing is unsound because `1916-03-30T23:30 → 1916-05-01T00:00` is zero months after the one-month landing normalizes to `00:30` and already overshoots. The residual day scan derives a complete post-floor fuel bound from civil-date distance and keeps fuel/profile failure as absence instead of collapsing it to zero. The day seed is semantically material, not just an optimization: a naïve full-range daily scan would retain clock adjustments from intervening gaps that the single bulk jump never visits; the leap-to-nonleap February-28 clock-reset case locks that separator. A separate before-anniversary case locks the completed-year candidate decrement. A March 30 `02:30` residual step lands at March 31 `01:30`; a second step retains `01:30`. Thus the `01:45` threshold counts one while elapsed-seconds division counts zero. Long-range controls are also essential: adjacent transition probes accidentally correlate source offset with direction, whereas a `2023-04-01T02:30 → 2024-04-05T02:30` span separates the source-offset account at 369 days from the former direction-selected 370. The year qualifier needs the same discipline: the tested one-year overlap shape self-corrects, while `2003-03-31T02:30 → 2024-03-31T03:00` separates compute-time gap resolution at 7670 from the former direction-selected 7671. Granularity is an independent reachability axis: before classifying a temporal landing rule as unreachable, enumerate transitions at the operand's time granularity rather than extrapolating from typical transition hours. The pinned Berlin table has no midnight gap but exactly one historical midnight overlap, 1 October 1916, so a Date-only month/year mutation can reach overlap resolution. Exact comparison against fresh construction turns that witness into independent semantic separators without another calendar mutation that could co-vary: retained day mutation is unequal, while later-resolved month and year mutation are equal. Re-resolution or earlier field landing reverses the corresponding result. The same mechanism covers the autumn fold, historical CEMT, target-date-preserving fallback, exact milliseconds, long ranges, and every transition in the pinned profile rather than a hard-coded date slice. One enum-based `ModelZone.ConcreteProfile` selector owns UTC/GMT/Berlin dispatch for `Today`, fresh-label resolution, and day difference; its staged `ofId?` plus typed evaluator distinguish unsupported selection from a supported Berlin gap, so the API cannot accidentally present Berlin as a general calendar account. Its exact-input form separately accepts an already-resolved local label and instant, preventing a stored overlap value from being re-resolved to the profile's fresh-label choice. Fresh construction and calendar shifts now share `ConstructedDateValue`, the generalized former shift-result carrier; it retains the selected instant, parts, omission, and formal cause, feeds shifts and differences directly, and projects into the same `SimpleComparisonOperand Instant` domain used by the existing exact temporal comparator. An Execute or Analyze consumer therefore does not need a private fixture equality or label reconstruction. The checked numeric atom uses the calendar route across validation, Number computation, arithmetic, and generated validation; it admits Date/DateTime/mixed field or direct/range-selected Base-Year operands, preserves formal cause before symmetric empty zero, and rejects unsupported profiles statically. Checked constructed-Date shifts and all three constructed-Date differences consume the selected UTC/GMT or Berlin profile without erasing reason-bearing non-values; pre-floor Berlin identity, recursive or wider operands, other zones, temporal targets, and retained project-local correspondence stay later consuming layers. The existing `CivilDate.unixEpochDay` remains a bound and inverse for its explicitly post-floor proleptic-Gregorian consumers, not a kernel-wide temporal quotient. a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` accepted the calendar-step implementation and adjacent kernel-route separators under [`SPEC-2026-07-20-08`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-20-08--differenceindays-counts-model-zone-calendar-steps); revisions `5716a964794e7b6e2e102ef56d898c5524f1d16a` and `17f95bcc7f0bd86b44413f1f41c5d2d351d84951` calibrate the later-discovered source-offset/refit day rule and sign-independent year-gap resolution. Revision `38b5046bc0c6cc26fcc4982dcb292b7b7822a981` confirms that the repeated-midnight comparison shapes are kernel-authorable only with quoted component constants and fixes the peer renderer that emitted the rejected bare form. Accepted revision `416065669ad177900e99e1dbec6cb53f3277252e` closes [`SPEC-2026-07-29-01`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-29-01): both kernel strategies plus dynamic/static/interpreter/JVM/Node paths lock the exact-instant `false/true/true` matrix and controls; it separately measures day-shift millisecond preservation and shows by mutation that February clock clearing is unobservable through the maintained whole-year consumer. This repository retains no portable Date observation. No harness, dependency, protocol, or generic calendar framework was added.

## LF48 — `FirstFilledValue` is a prefix-sensitive scan, not a list fold

> Date: 2026-07-20. Sections: §3, §8, §10, §11. Basis: [`spec/03-empty-and-required.md`](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md), [`spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md`](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md), [`spec/09-computations.md`](../spec/09-computations.md), and [`spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md`](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md); pinned kernel 30.8.1 [`FirstValueCombiner.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/FirstValueCombiner.java), `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe`, `CheckFeldListenOpImpl`, and `CheckEntityListenUtils` at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; read-only a12-dmkits `FirstFilledValue` laws, differentials, and current aggregate authoring at revisions `3696df4e38ae6f13fb97c2bddfcdf2cc730c4869` and `132bf3bd984615b4373d3d6bd18c67746cc5673b`; red/green suffix-poison, empty-prefix, all-empty-identity, filtered-empty, lazy-topology, checker-priority, and wildcard-duplicate separators; independent semantic and Lean reviews; trusted scan/projection and checked-source laws.

`FirstFilledValue` consumes an ordered prefix, not a complete multiset. It skips empties until either a present cell supplies the result or a formally unavailable cell makes the result unavailable. Either event terminates the scan: an invalid cell before the first value suppresses validation and poisons computation, while the same invalid cell after a selected value is never read. An actual empty cell before selection does not change the eventual amount but remains observable; an empty after selection is invisible. Exhausting an explicitly empty, marked-uninstantiated, or filtered-empty Number selection supplies zero rather than no value.

The empty prefix affects consumers asymmetrically. Validation receives the selected amount with bidirectional fillability, because another filled choice can replace the current first value, so a firing can become OMISSION-typed. Without that prefix the same selected amount is fixed and the corresponding firing is VALUE-typed. Computation receives the amount in both cases. A resolved `Having` filter conservatively applies the same not-given/fillable classification even when its selected cell is present. Source inspection alone initially suggested that the combiner's separate `omittedTail` state would not mark a value selected from a later operand not-given. Kernel execution disproved that inference: `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` presents a reached flat or nested star with no concrete cell through the ordinary not-given value path before the fallback, so the wrapper has already established the sticky empty prefix; `omittedTail` still affects the combiner's all-exhausted identity separately. Filter encounter is also ordered across authored operand slots: the kernel marks a slot's filter immediately before consuming that slot and never sees a later slot after termination.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/FirstFilledValue.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/FirstFilledValue.lean) reuses the already-expanded Number cell and side types from the value-list capsule and scans a nonempty list of resolved operand slots. The shared state enters each slot lazily, retains encountered filters, actual empty cells, and reached cell-free selections as the same observable prefix for a later value, and retains omitted tails separately for the all-exhausted identity. `FirstFilledNumberResult` keeps `value amount notGiven | unavailable cause`, then projects independently to `NumericOperand` and `NumericComputationResult`. [`Elaboration/FirstFilledValue.lean`](../A12Kernel/Elaboration/FirstFilledValue.lean) resolves mixed direct/plain-star/filtered-star references before applying the kernel's direct-only duplicate, cardinality, and common Number-kind gates, then lazily resolves only reached runtime slots through one continuation-capable relevance/classification scan. Wildcarded slots are intentionally not deduplicated. [`Proofs/FirstFilledValue.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/FirstFilledValue.lean) proves the scan laws plus checked cardinality and direct-reference uniqueness. [`Conformance/FirstFilledValue.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/FirstFilledValue.lean) and [`Conformance/FirstFilledValueElaboration.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/FirstFilledValueElaboration.lean) lock the polarity, poison, flat and nested no-row behavior, authoring priority, scale, wildcard repetition, filter, relevance, and lazy-topology matrix.

Limit: the checked mixed route is Number- and validation-specific. The later addressed route integrates it with the closed checked document and computation for its admitted Number family; group operands, Date, DateRange, broader token identities, target/application breadth, messages, the SG4 result boundary, protocol exposure, and local portable evidence remain outside. Exact formal-cause carriage is an internal refinement. a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` accepted ordered filter encounter under [`SPEC-2026-07-20-09`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-20-09--firstfilledvalue-observes-only-filters-before-termination); revision `83dd514f9283b9f62dbe6ee6f238e5c67a00e9c6` rejected [`SPEC-2026-07-22-06`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-22-06--firstfilledvalue-keeps-omitted-tail-distinct-from-an-empty-prefix) with flat and nested kernel differentials and caused the corresponding Lean correction; revision `d82260d87de0c3e4576895b665819d205756f857` accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-22-07`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-22-07--ordinary-aggregate-duplicate-checking-skips-wildcarded-operands). Broader correspondence remains `external evidence pending`, and this repository retains no portable `FirstFilledValue` observation. No support, evidence, protocol, harness, governance, dependency, or aggregate framework was added.

## LF49 — Date-range overlap is closed, occurrence-based, and shape-sensitive

> Date: 2026-07-20. Sections: §6 and §12. Basis: [`spec/05-dates-and-time.md` §8](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#8-date-ranges-and-overlap); pinned kernel 30.8.1 `DatumBereich.ueberlappt`, `RuntimeController.datumsBereicheUeberlappend`, and `mindestensEinDatumsBereichUeberlappendFL` at revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`; read-only a12-dmkits overlap evaluators and maintained `DateRangeOverlapSemanticsDiffTest`/`DateRangeDiffTest` at revision `6f062a0d001e6140103e1acedbf00ff7ee512189`; red/green conformance plus inversion-guard, half-open-endpoint, scalar-versus-list, lost-sticky-filter, filtered-current-marker-late, skipped-scalar-rescue, and scalar-filter-taint mutations; independent semantic, Lean, and consumer review; trusted direction, overlap, symmetry, invalidity, gap, occurrence, non-UNKNOWN, skipped-inertness, first-match, and polarity laws.

The primitive relation is a closed interval relation: two supplied endpoint pairs overlap exactly when neither is inverted and neither finish lies strictly before the other start. Equal endpoints therefore form a valid one-day range, and ranges sharing one endpoint overlap. Inversion is not normalized by swapping endpoints; it returns false. A stored inverted DateRange is normally formal-invalid and skipped before this relation, but the runtime helper independently retains the defensive false branch if inversion reaches it.

`DateRangesOverlap` is not a distinguished scalar checked against a list. It scans one growing collection of every kept, filled occurrence from every authored operand. Occurrence identity matters: one range occurrence cannot pair with itself, the same range arriving through scalar and star is two occurrences and does pair, and a disjoint first occurrence cannot hide an overlapping pair later in the stream. `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` has a different shape: its scalar must be available, it compares that scalar only with list occurrences, and internal list overlap cannot rescue an unavailable scalar. Their polarity mechanisms differ again. Any-pair remembers whether a kept, filled occurrence from a reached filtered operand has been processed before the first match; scalar-versus-list derives polarity only from the matched list container. Flattened truth alone cannot recover either provenance rule.

Lean treatment: [`Semantics/DateRangeOverlap.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateRangeOverlap.lean) uses `DateRangeDirection` to make ordered versus inverted supplied endpoints explicit, reuses `FullDate.before` for the closed primitive relation, and scans a `List ResolvedDateRange` so duplicate occurrences remain observable. The forward current-versus-suffix recursion is truth-equivalent to the kernel's current-versus-previously-seen scan because [`Proofs/DateRangeOverlap.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/DateRangeOverlap.lean) proves primitive symmetry. It deliberately carries no filter or operand provenance and is therefore retained only as the truth projection, not reused as the polarity-bearing operator scan. [`Conformance/DateRangeOverlap.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/DateRangeOverlap.lean) locks touching versus adjacency, containment, both inversion positions, singleton versus duplicate occurrences, a later internal pair, and a pairwise-disjoint control.

[`Semantics/DateRangeOverlapOperators.lean`](../A12Kernel/Semantics/DateRangeOverlapOperators.lean) then retains ordered authored operand boundaries as skipped/kept slots plus filter presence. The any-pair evaluator scans current occurrences against the previous kept prefix and makes its filter marker sticky only after a kept occurrence is reached; the scalar-versus-list evaluator keeps the scalar separate, returns immediately when it is skipped, and derives polarity only from the first matching list operand. [`Proofs/DateRangeOverlapOperators.lean`](../A12Kernel/Proofs/DateRangeOverlapOperators.lean) proves that neither resolved operator yields UNKNOWN, that skipped filtered input is inert, that two-occurrence and one-occurrence shapes delegate to primitive overlap, and that sticky-prefix versus matched-container polarity remain distinct. Its two universal truth-forgetting bridges prove that operator grouping and filter provenance affect firing polarity but not whether the corresponding flat primitive relation has a witness; the any-pair bridge explicitly reconciles the evaluator's prefix scan with the truth capsule's suffix scan through primitive symmetry. [`Conformance/DateRangeOverlapOperators.lean`](../A12Kernel/Conformance/DateRangeOverlapOperators.lean) locks later internal pairs, duplicate occurrences, filtered skipped input, reached disjoint filters, a filtered current match, unreachable later filters, reordering-sensitive polarity, skipped/disjoint scalars, and both matched-container polarities.

Limit: this begins after endpoint parsing/calendar admission, formal checking, partial-relevance classification, and actual `Having` selection. A skipped slot deliberately collapses empty, formally unavailable, and filter-dropped cells because all are inert to these two consuming scans; it is not a new cell model. It does not implement fragment-range completion, Base Year, raw DateRange formats/separators, field reads, filter evaluation or `$`, row gates, partial-validation orchestration, paths/stars, equality/inequality, DateRange construction/extraction, messages, checked lowering, protocol exposure, or local portable evidence. a12-dmkits revision `71775c9905b057831253348c31ce39e321e61889` accepted and locked the reached-scan polarity and scalar early-exit behavior under [`SPEC-2026-07-20-10`](archived/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER-THROUGH-2026-08-28.md#spec-2026-07-20-10--date-range-overlap-polarity-follows-the-reached-scan), but this repository retains no portable Date-range observation, so correspondence remains `external evidence pending`. No support, evidence, protocol, harness, governance, dependency, or generic overlap framework was added.

## LF50 — computation field-fill quantifiers are ordered phase-specific scans

> Date: 2026-07-20. Sections: §1, §3, §11.
